用于 TTI 筛查的化学发光免疫分析和核酸检测:性能、互补性和血液安全性的叙述性回顾

Chemiluminescence immunoassay and nucleic acid testing for TTI screening: A narrative review of performance, complementarity, and blood safety
作者： Abdul Karim
摘要中文翻译简介:输血传播感染 (TTI) 筛查对于血液安全至关重要。化学发光免疫分析 (ChLIA) 可实现自动化、高通量血清学筛查,而核酸检测 (NAT) 可检测病毒核酸并缩短诊断窗口期。本综述旨在评估 ChLIA 筛查 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV)、丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV)、人类免疫缺陷病毒 (HIV) 和梅毒的效果;将其与 NAT 对 HBV、HCV 和 HIV 的性能进行比较;方法:由一位评审员 (A.K.) 进行定向叙述评审。PubMed 于 2026 年 9 月 7 日使用预定义的、可重复的搜索策略进行了搜索。补充检索包括同行评审期刊、参考文献列表、世界卫生组织出版物和印度尼西亚官方监管来源。根据与献血者筛查、ChLIA-NAT 比较、残余输血风险、隐匿性 HBV 感染和印度尼西亚监管要求的相关性,对研究进行优先排序。不确定的记录进行了全文评估。结果:纳入的研究在测定特征、筛选标记、供体群体和参考标准方面各不相同,排除了诊断准确性估计的有意义的汇总或概括。ChLIA 和 NAT 检测不同的生物靶标,其对血液安全的相对贡献受病原体 流行病学、残留风险、监管要求和医疗资源的影响。Shahin 等人的研究结果。印度尼西亚的研究提供了各自的分母和不一致的测试模式,以确保正确的解释。结论:ChLIA 表现出较高的诊断性能,尽管准确性因检测和临床环境而异。在病原体特异性残留风险、当地 流行病学、监管要求和医疗保健能力的指导下,它与 NAT 的补充使用可以提高输血安全性。这两种方法都应整合到全面的质量保证和血液警戒系统中。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
输血传播感染 (TTI) 筛查对于血液安全至关重要。本综述旨在评估 ChLIA 筛查 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV)、丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV)、人类免疫缺陷病毒 (HIV) 和梅毒的效果;将其与 NAT 对 HBV、HCV 和 HIV 的性能进行比较;并检查对输血安全的影响。
证据基础与方法
化学发光免疫分析 (ChLIA) 可实现自动化、高通量血清学筛查,而核酸检测 (NAT) 可检测病毒核酸并缩短诊断窗口期。PubMed 于 2026 年 9 月 7 日使用预定义的、可重复的搜索策略进行了搜索。补充检索包括同行评审期刊、参考文献列表、世界卫生组织出版物和印度尼西亚官方监管来源。不确定的记录进行了全文评估。
共识与结论
尽管准确性因检测和临床环境而异,但 ChLIA 表现出较高的诊断性能。在病原体特异性残留风险、当地 流行病学、监管要求和医疗保健能力的指导下,它与 NAT 的补充使用可以提高输血安全性。这两种方法都应整合到全面的质量保证和血液警戒系统中。
争议与缺口
纳入的研究在测定特征、筛选标记、供体群体和参考标准方面各不相同,排除了诊断准确性估计的有意义的汇集或概括。Shahin 等人的研究结果。和印度尼西亚的研究都提供了各自的分母和不一致的测试模式,以确保适当的解释。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调需要将 ChLIA 和 NAT 整合到全面的质量保证和血液警戒系统中。

Chemiluminescence immunoassay and nucleic acid testing for TTI screening: A narrative review of performance, complementarity, and blood safety

Authors: Abdul Karim
Original abstract (English)
Introduction: Screening for transfusion-transmitted infections (TTIs) is essential to blood safety. Chemiluminescence immunoassay (ChLIA) enables automated, high-throughput serological screening, whereas nucleic acid testing (NAT) detects viral nucleic acids and reduces the diagnostic window period. This review aimed to evaluate ChLIA for screening hepatitis B virus (HBV), hepatitis C virus (HCV), human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), and syphilis; compare its performance with NAT for HBV, HCV, and HIV; and examine the implications for transfusion safety.Methods: A directed narrative review was conducted by a single reviewer (A.K.). PubMed was searched on September 7, 2026, using a predefined, reproducible search strategy. Supplementary searches included peer-reviewed journals, reference lists, World Health Organization publications, and official Indonesian regulatory sources. Studies were prioritized according to their relevance to blood donor screening, ChLIA–NAT comparisons, residual transfusion risk, occult HBV infection, and Indonesian regulatory requirements. Uncertain records underwent full-text assessment.Results: The included studies varied in assay characteristics, screening markers, donor populations, and reference standards, precluding meaningful pooling or generalization of diagnostic accuracy estimates. ChLIA and NAT detect distinct biological targets, with their relative contributions to blood safety influenced by pathogen epidemiology, residual risk, regulatory requirements, and healthcare resources. Findings from Shahin et al. and the Indonesian study are presented with their respective denominators and discordant testing patterns to ensure appropriate interpretation.Conclusion: ChLIA demonstrates high diagnostic performance, although accuracy varies across assays and clinical settings. Its complementary use with NAT may enhance transfusion safety when guided by pathogen-specific residual risk, local epidemiology, regulatory requirements, and healthcare capacity. Both approaches should be integrated within comprehensive quality assurance and hemovigilance systems.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Screening for transfusion-transmitted infections (TTIs) is essential to blood safety. This review aimed to evaluate ChLIA for screening hepatitis B virus (HBV), hepatitis C virus (HCV), human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), and syphilis; compare its performance with NAT for HBV, HCV, and HIV; and examine the implications for transfusion safety.
Evidence base and method
Chemiluminescence immunoassay (ChLIA) enables automated, high-throughput serological screening, whereas nucleic acid testing (NAT) detects viral nucleic acids and reduces the diagnostic window period. PubMed was searched on September 7, 2026, using a predefined, reproducible search strategy. Supplementary searches included peer-reviewed journals, reference lists, World Health Organization publications, and official Indonesian regulatory sources. Uncertain records underwent full-text assessment.
Consensus and conclusions
ChLIA demonstrates high diagnostic performance, although accuracy varies across assays and clinical settings. Its complementary use with NAT may enhance transfusion safety when guided by pathogen-specific residual risk, local epidemiology, regulatory requirements, and healthcare capacity. Both approaches should be integrated within comprehensive quality assurance and hemovigilance systems.
Controversies and gaps
The included studies varied in assay characteristics, screening markers, donor populations, and reference standards, precluding meaningful pooling or generalization of diagnostic accuracy estimates. Findings from Shahin et al. and the Indonesian study are presented with their respective denominators and discordant testing patterns to ensure appropriate interpretation.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for integrating ChLIA and NAT within comprehensive quality assurance and hemovigilance systems.

巴基斯坦普通人群乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎的 患病率:2010-2025 年血清学和活动性感染的系统回顾和荟萃分析

Prevalence of hepatitis B and hepatitis C in the general population of Pakistan: a systematic review and meta-analysis of serological and active infection, 2010–2025
作者： Abdullah Abdullah, Muhammad Salman
摘要中文翻译这项系统回顾和荟萃分析将估计 2010 年至 2025 年巴基斯坦普通人群中乙型肝炎 (HBsAg) 和丙型肝炎(抗 HCV 和 HCV RNA)的汇总 患病率。HBsAg、抗 HCV 和 HCV RNA 将分别汇总。该综述将研究患病率如何随省份、时间段(2010-2015年、2016-2020年、2021-2025年)、性别、年龄组、城市/农村环境和诊断方法的变化而变化。仅纳入一般人群研究。献血者、血液透析、地中海贫血、仅限孕妇、囚犯、注射毒品和肝病选择的样本将被排除在外。数据库:PubMed/MEDLINE、Scopus、Embase、Web of Science、PakMediNet 和 Google Scholar。两名独立评审员将使用 JBI 患病率 研究清单进行筛选、提取数据并评估质量。分析将使用 R 中的随机效应模型。报告将遵循 PRISMA 2020。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
这项系统回顾和荟萃分析将估计 2010 年至 2025 年巴基斯坦普通人群中乙型肝炎 (HBsAg) 和丙型肝炎(抗 HCV 和 HCV RNA)的汇总 患病率。
证据基础与方法
HBsAg、抗 HCV 和 HCV RNA 将分别合并。数据库:PubMed/MEDLINE、Scopus、Embase、Web of Science、PakMediNet 和 Google Scholar。两名独立评审员将使用 JBI 患病率 研究清单进行筛选、提取数据并评估质量。分析将使用 R 中的随机效应模型。报告将遵循 PRISMA 2020。
共识与结论
该综述将研究患病率如何随省份、时间段(2010-2015年、2016-2020年、2021-2025年)、性别、年龄组、城市/农村环境和诊断方法的变化而变化。
争议与缺口
仅纳入一般人群研究。献血者、血液透析、地中海贫血、仅限孕妇、囚犯、注射毒品和肝病选择的样本将被排除在外。
科研与实践启示
该综述将深入了解巴基斯坦乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎 患病率 的地理和时间变化,从而为公共卫生战略和干预措施提供信息。

Prevalence of hepatitis B and hepatitis C in the general population of Pakistan: a systematic review and meta-analysis of serological and active infection, 2010–2025

Authors: Abdullah Abdullah, Muhammad Salman
Original abstract (English)
This systematic review and meta-analysis will estimate the pooled prevalence of hepatitis B (HBsAg) and hepatitis C (anti-HCV and HCV RNA) in the general population of Pakistan from 2010 to 2025. HBsAg, anti-HCV and HCV RNA will be pooled separately. The review will examine how prevalence varies by province, time period (2010-2015, 2016-2020, 2021-2025), sex, age group, urban/rural setting, and diagnostic method. Only general-population studies will be included. Blood donors, haemodialysis, thalassaemia, pregnant-women-only, prisoner, injecting drug use and liver-disease-selected samples will be excluded. Databases: PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Embase, Web of Science, PakMediNet and Google Scholar. Two independent reviewers will screen, extract data and assess quality using the JBI Checklist for Prevalence Studies. Analysis will use a random-effects model in R. Reporting will follow PRISMA 2020.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review and meta-analysis will estimate the pooled prevalence of hepatitis B (HBsAg) and hepatitis C (anti-HCV and HCV RNA) in the general population of Pakistan from 2010 to 2025.
Evidence base and method
HBsAg, anti-HCV and HCV RNA will be pooled separately. Databases: PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Embase, Web of Science, PakMediNet and Google Scholar. Two independent reviewers will screen, extract data and assess quality using the JBI Checklist for Prevalence Studies. Analysis will use a random-effects model in R. Reporting will follow PRISMA 2020.
Consensus and conclusions
The review will examine how prevalence varies by province, time period (2010-2015, 2016-2020, 2021-2025), sex, age group, urban/rural setting, and diagnostic method.
Controversies and gaps
Only general-population studies will be included. Blood donors, haemodialysis, thalassaemia, pregnant-women-only, prisoner, injecting drug use and liver-disease-selected samples will be excluded.
Research and practice implications
The review will provide insights into the geographical and temporal variations in hepatitis B and C prevalence in Pakistan, which can inform public health strategies and interventions.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

另一种药物在 慢性乙型肝炎 中显示出 功能性治愈 的潜力 - MedPage Today

Another Drug Shows Potential for Functional Cures in Chronic Hepatitis B - MedPage Today
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 10 日在丹佛举行的美国肝病研究协会年会上公布的一项 II 期研究评估了 siRNA 疗法 HRS-5635 治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 感染的效果。该试验涉及中国 32 名接受稳定核(酸)类似物治疗的 HBV 感染患者。所有患者的 HBsAg 水平均超过 100 且低于 3,000 IU/mL,HBV DNA 水平低于 20 IU/mL。HRS-5635 的最高剂量(400 mg)显示,治疗 结束后 24 周,19% 的患者出现 功能性治愈,定义为持续 HBsAg 清除率低于 0.05 IU/mL。该疗法总体上是安全且耐受性良好的,仅发生了两例与 治疗 相关的 3 级或以上不良事件,并且没有严重不良事件或死亡。研究发现 抗病毒药物 反应呈剂量依赖性,所有四种 HRS-5635 剂量均可降低 HBsAg 水平。该研究结果基于二期试验,尚未得到更广泛应用的证实。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究结果于2026年11月10日在丹佛举行的美国肝病研究协会年会上报告。治疗治疗期持续48周,治疗治疗后24周进行随访。
地点与对象
该试验在中国进行,涉及接受稳定核苷类似物治疗的 慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 感染患者。参与者的 HBsAg 水平超过 100 且低于 3,000 IU/mL,HBV DNA 水平低于 20 IU/mL。大多数 HBeAg 阴性(65.6% 至 73.3%)并且没有肝硬化。
事件
一项 II 期研究评估了 siRNA 疗法 HRS-5635 治疗慢性 HBV 感染的效果。治疗 结束后 24 周,最高剂量(400 mg)显示 19% 的患者出现 功能性治愈,HBsAg 水平低于 0.05 IU/mL。该研究已在 AASLD 年会上发表。
规模、影响与风险
在接受最高剂量的 32 名患者中,19% 在 治疗 治疗后 24 周达到了 功能性治愈。研究发现 抗病毒药物 反应呈剂量依赖性,所有四种 HRS-5635 剂量均可降低 HBsAg 水平。该疗法总体上是安全且耐受性良好的,仅发生了两例与 治疗 相关的 3 级或以上不良事件,并且没有严重不良事件或死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究结果在 AASLD 年会上报告,该疗法被描述为总体安全且耐受性良好。功能性治愈 被定义为 治疗 后 24 周持续 HBV DNA 抑制和 HBsAg 清除。该研究结果基于二期试验,尚未得到更广泛应用的证实。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Another Drug Shows Potential for Functional Cures in Chronic Hepatitis B - MedPage Today

News Brief
A phase II study presented at the American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases annual meeting in Denver on November 10, 2026, evaluated the siRNA therapy HRS-5635 for treating chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection. The trial involved 32 patients in China with HBV infection who were on stable nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy. All patients had HBsAg levels over 100 and less than 3,000 IU/mL, and HBV DNA levels below 20 IU/mL. The highest dose of HRS-5635 (400 mg) showed a functional cure in 19% of patients 24 weeks after treatment ended, defined as sustained HBsAg clearance below 0.05 IU/mL. The therapy was generally safe and well-tolerated, with only two treatment-related adverse events of grade 3 or higher and no serious adverse events or deaths. The study found a dose-dependent antiviral response, with all four HRS-5635 doses reducing HBsAg levels. The findings are based on a phase II trial and are not yet confirmed for broader application.
Original source text (English)

Meeting Coverage > AASLD Another Drug Shows Potential for Functional Cures in Chronic Hepatitis B — With highest dose of siRNA therapy, 19% achieved a functional cure 24 weeks after treatment ended by Terrence Rudd, Staff Writer, MedPage Today November 10, 2026 • 3 min read Add MedPage Today on Google MedpageToday Reductions in hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) were observed with a novel small interfering RNA (siRNA) therapy in patients with chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, according to a phase II study. Among patients randomized to monotherapy with one of four doses of the siRNA HRS-5635, all 32 on the highest dose (400 mg) had HBsAg levels less than 100 IU/mL after 48 weeks of treatment, with 88% still at that level 24 weeks after treatment ended. Among this high-dose group, 16% achieved a functional cure, with an HBsAg clearance level of less than 0.05 IU/mL after 48 weeks -- a percentage that rose to 19% by 24 weeks post-treatment, reported Xieer Liang, PhD, of Southern Medical University in Guangzhou, China, at the American Association for the Study of Liver Diseasesopens in a new tab or window annual meeting in Denver. "A dose-dependent antiviral response was observed with HRS-5635," Liang said. "All HRS-5635 regimens were generally safe and well-tolerated." An estimated 240 million people worldwide have chronic HBV infection, including about 640,000 U.S. adultsopens in a new tab or window. Chronic HBV can cause liver damage, cirrhosis, liver cancer, and death. Five antivirals and two immune modulators are approved in the U.S. opens in a new tab or windowto treat chronic HBV, with treatment regimens lasting from 6 months to over 1 year. In a previous phase II trialopens in a new tab or window, siRNA therapy with an immunomodulator reduced HBsAg in patients with chronic HBV infection. Last month, the FDA approved another nucleic acid-based therapeutic, the antisense oligonucleotide drug bepirovirsen (Hibsago)opens in a new tab or window, for patients with chronic HBV taking antiviral therapy based on results from the B-Well 1 and B-Well 2opens in a new tab or window studies. In those trials, 19% to 20% of patients with HBsAg levels between 100 and 3,000 IU/mL had a functional cure with bepirovirsen at week 72. The current trial included patients with chronic HBV on stable nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy who had HBsAg levels over 100 and less than 3,000 IU/mL and HBV DNA levels less than 20 IU/mL. Patients could be hepatitis B envelope antigen (HBeAg) positive or negative, but they couldn't have cirrhosis. All patients received tenofovir amibufenamide during 48 weeks of HRS-5635 treatment. Mean age ranged from 41.5 to 44.6 years in the four treatment groups, 59.4% to 76.7% were men, and mean HBsAg ranged from 1,058.2 IU/mL to 1,200.4 IU/mL. Most patients were HBeAg negative (65.6% to 73.3%). The investigators defined functional cure as sustained HBV DNA suppression and HBsAg clearance of less than 0.05 IU/mL, with or without seroconversion, sustained for 24 weeks after treatment. Medical News from Around the Web 1 NBC News She’s delivered 2,000 babies. The measles outbreak changed her mind about vaccines. opens in a new tab or window 2 Time Weight-Loss Drugs May Help Sleep Apnea and Arthritis opens in a new tab or window 3 NPR How word of mouth is helping an Amish community's fight against measles opens in a new tab or window 4 Clinical Advisor New Mother's Death Adds to Measles Toll as Pregnancy Warnings Grow opens in a new tab or window 5 CBS News Dubious buys raise alarm over local control of opioid cash opens in a new tab or window All four HRS-5635 doses achieved the study's primary endpoint of HBsAg reduction from baseline to week 12, with mean reductions of -1.26 log10 IU/mL in the 50-mg group, -1.90 log10 IU/mL in the 100-mg group, -1.99 log10 IU/mL in the 200-mg group, and -2.12 log10 IU/mL in the 400-mg group. Among patients in the 400-mg group, 45% converted from HBeAg positive to HBeAg negative after 48 weeks of treatment, a percentage that slid to 27% at 24 weeks after treatment. In patients receiving the 400-mg dose who were HBeAg negative and had HBsAg below 1,000 IU/mL at baseline, 33% reached the functional cure rate of HBsAg less than 0.05 IU/mL. Across the four groups, 46.9% to 60% had a treatment-related adverse event. There were only two such events that were grade 3 or higher, and no serious adverse events related to treatment and no deaths occurred. Upper respiratory tract infections were the most common adverse event, occurring in 25% to 33.3% of patients in the four groups. Alanine transaminase elevations occurred in up to 6.7% of patients during and after treatment, Liang said, but levels were generally stable during treatment, and no elevations reached a grade 3 level. The follow-up period of 24 weeks after treatment ended was relatively short, Liang cautioned, but a longer-duration extension study is ongoing, along with a phase III trialopens in a new tab or window. The study results were reported at the American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases annual meeting in Denver on November 10, 2026. The treatment period lasted 48 weeks, with follow-up at 24 weeks post-treatment.

The trial was conducted in China, involving patients with chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection who were on stable nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy. Participants had HBsAg levels over 100 and less than 3,000 IU/mL and HBV DNA levels less than 20 IU/mL. Most were HBeAg negative (65.6% to 73.3%) and did not have cirrhosis.
Event
A phase II study evaluated the siRNA therapy HRS-5635 for treating chronic HBV infection. The highest dose (400 mg) showed a functional cure in 19% of patients 24 weeks after treatment ended, with HBsAg levels below 0.05 IU/mL. The study was presented at the AASLD annual meeting.
Scale, impact and risk
Among the 32 patients on the highest dose, 19% achieved a functional cure 24 weeks post-treatment. The therapy was generally safe and well-tolerated, with only two treatment-related adverse events of grade 3 or higher and no serious adverse events or deaths.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study results were reported at the AASLD annual meeting, and the therapy was described as generally safe and well-tolerated. The functional cure was defined as sustained HBV DNA suppression and HBsAg clearance for 24 weeks post-treatment. The findings are based on a phase II trial and are not yet confirmed for broader application.

美国肝移植受者中乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 发病率不理想 - 传染病顾问

Suboptimal Hepatitis B Vaccination Rates in US Liver Transplant Recipients - Infectious Disease Advisor
新闻简报The article published on 2026-11-09 by Infectious Disease Advisor highlights suboptimal Hepatitis B vaccination rates among liver transplant recipients in the United States. It reports that this issue is a concern for public health, as it may increase the risk of Hepatitis B infection and its complications in this vulnerable population. The article does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers but emphasizes the importance of addressing vaccination gaps. The information is based on expert insights and does not mention any specific investigation or intervention measures. The source is a reputable medical publication, and the findings are not confirmed as an outbreak but rather as a reported issue requiring attention.

时间
这篇文章于2026-11-09发表。它讨论了一项在美国进行的研究,但没有提供具体事件日期。
地点与对象

事件

规模、影响与风险
该文章没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。它的重点是低 疫苗接种 率对弱势群体的影响,这可能会增加乙型肝炎感染及其并发症的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章将这一问题视为公共卫生问题,并强调需要改进 疫苗接种 实践。它没有提及任何具体的调查、测试或干预措施。来源是著名的医学出版物,信息基于专家的见解。

基因编辑消除每位目标剂量患者体内的 储存宿主 病毒后,乙型肝炎治愈更接近 - Tech Times

Hepatitis B Cure Closer After Gene Editing Eliminates Viral Reservoir in Every Target-Dose Patient - Tech Times
新闻简报2026 年 9 月 28 日在科罗拉多州丹佛市举行的美国肝病研究协会 (AASLD) The Liver Meeting® 2026 上发表的一项开创性研究表明,使用 Precision BioSciences 的 PBGENE-HBV 疗法进行基因编辑可以消除 92% 具有可检测到的前基因组 RNA (pgRNA) 的患者中的乙型肝炎病毒 储存宿主 (共价闭合环状DNA)。该试验涉及 25 名患者,在四个剂量水平上总共给药 59 次。在 12 名开始时可检测到 pgRNA 的患者中,有 11 名达到了不可检测的 pgRNA 水平,其中包括 0.4 mg/kg 剂量下的所有 6 名可评估患者。这是迄今为止针对乙型肝炎的体内基因编辑最广泛的人体证据。该疗法基于 ARCUS 平台,使用单链单体蛋白直接靶向 HBV 聚合酶基因,与现有的 抗病毒药物 治疗 相比,提供了更精确且潜在持久的解决方案。结果表明,消除 共价闭合环状DNA 可以降低再激活的风险,但这些结果是初步的,需要进一步的研究来确认长期疗效和安全性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 9 月 28 日在科罗拉多州丹佛市举行的美国肝病研究协会 (AASLD) The Liver Meeting® 2026 上发表。数据截止日期为 2026 年 9 月 28 日,试验开始于在此日期之前接受 治疗 的患者。
地点与对象
该研究涉及 25 名 慢性乙型肝炎 患者,特别是那些在试验开始时可检测到前基因组 RNA (pgRNA) 的患者。地点没有明确说明,但试验是在美国进行的,因为该公司总部位于北卡罗来纳州达勒姆。
事件
Precision BioSciences 公布了 ELIMINATE-B 研究的 1 期数据,显示使用 PBGENE-HBV 进行基因编辑可以消除 92% 具有可检测 pgRNA 的患者中的病毒 储存宿主 (共价闭合环状DNA),其中包括 0.4 mg/kg 剂量水平的所有 6 名可评估患者。这被认为是这种方法迄今为止最完整的人类证据。
规模、影响与风险
该研究涉及 25 名患者,在四个剂量水平上总共给药 59 次。结果表明,12 名可检测到 pgRNA 的患者中有 11 名 (92%) 达到了不可检测的 pgRNA 水平,表明对病毒 储存宿主 产生了显着影响。如果 共价闭合环状DNA 被消除,乙型肝炎重新激活的风险就会降低,因为它是新病毒颗粒的来源。
应对、状态与不确定性
The study is in Phase 1, and the results are based on data up to September 28, 2026. The therapy is being advanced to a global Phase 2 study. The findings are based on a molecular readout (pgRNA) and are considered preliminary, as the long-term effects and cure status are not yet confirmed.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B Cure Closer After Gene Editing Eliminates Viral Reservoir in Every Target-Dose Patient - Tech Times

News Brief
A groundbreaking study presented at the American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases (AASLD) The Liver Meeting® 2026 in Denver, Colorado, on September 28, 2026, shows that gene editing using Precision BioSciences' PBGENE-HBV therapy can eliminate the hepatitis B viral reservoir (cccDNA) in 92% of patients with detectable pregenomic RNA (pgRNA). The trial involved 25 patients, with 59 total drug administrations across four dose levels. Among 12 patients with detectable pgRNA at the start, 11 achieved undetectable pgRNA levels, including all six evaluable patients at the 0.4 mg/kg dose. This is the most extensive human evidence to date for in vivo gene editing targeting hepatitis B. The therapy, based on the ARCUS platform, uses a single-chain monomeric protein to directly target the HBV polymerase gene, offering a more precise and potentially durable solution compared to existing antiviral treatments. The results suggest that eliminating cccDNA could reduce the risk of reactivation, but the findings are preliminary, and further studies are needed to confirm long-term efficacy and safety.
Original source text (English)
A nurse loads a syringe with a vaccine against hepatitis at a free immunization clinic for students before the start of the school year, in Lynwood, California August, 27, 2013. Nurses are immunizing children in preparation for the first day of public school on September 3. The clinic offers the mandatory vaccinations for school children against diphtheria, tetanus, pertussis, polio, hepatitis B, MMR (measles, mumps, rubella) and chickenpox as well as some optional ones. AFP PHOTO / Robyn Beck ROBYN BECK/AFP via Getty Images For the roughly 254 million people living with chronic hepatitis B worldwide, every approved therapy amounts to a managed retreat. Antiviral drugs suppress the virus but rarely vanquish it, and patients typically face a lifetime of daily pills. The reason no treatment has achieved a true cure in more than four decades of trying comes down to a single molecular adversary: covalently closed circular DNA , or cccDNA — a tightly wound viral genome that hides inside the nuclei of infected liver cells and continues churning out new virus particles even as antivirals hold the outer infection in check. Today, at the American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases (AASLD) The Liver Meeting® 2026 in Denver, Colorado, Durham-based biotechnology company Precision BioSciences presented the most complete human evidence yet that gene editing can eliminate that reservoir — and do it durably. The stakes extend beyond "cure" in the conventional sense. Even patients who achieve what hepatologists call a functional cure — the loss of detectable hepatitis B surface antigen — still carry residual cccDNA in liver tissue. That dormant reservoir can reactivate when immunity wanes, posing an underappreciated danger for the hundreds of thousands of chronic HBV patients who each year require chemotherapy, organ transplantation, or biological therapies. A therapy that eliminates cccDNA would address the root cause of that reactivation risk, not merely its clinical readout. A Late-Breaking Moment in Denver The late-breaking oral presentation, delivered at 2:15 p.m. MST by lead investigator Dr. Man-Fung Yuen — Chair Professor at The University of Hong Kong and Chief of the Division of Gastroenterology and Hepatology at Queen Mary Hospital — covers updated Phase 1 data from the ELIMINATE-B study ( NCT06680232 on ClinicalTrials.gov ) of PBGENE-HBV , Precision's wholly owned in vivo gene editing program. The dataset is the most expansive yet reported for any in vivo gene editing approach targeting hepatitis B: 25 patients, 59 total drug administrations, four dose levels, and a data cutoff of September 28, 2026 . The headline result: among 12 patients with detectable pregenomic RNA (pgRNA) at the start of the trial, 11 (92%) reached undetectable pgRNA following treatment — including all six evaluable patients dosed at the 0.4 mg/kg level that Precision has selected to advance into a global Phase 2 study. Why pgRNA — and Why It Matters pgRNA, or pregenomic RNA, is a transcript produced exclusively from cccDNA. That exclusivity makes it unusually informative as a biomarker: when pgRNA disappears from a patient's blood, the most straightforward explanation is that the cccDNA driving its production has been eliminated or permanently silenced in the liver. It is, in essence, a molecular readout of the gene edit working as designed. The clinical significance of that readout was quantified in a landmark study: Terrault et al. found that patients with undetectable pgRNA at the time of nucleoside/nucleotide analog (NUC) therapy discontinuation have an approximately 10-fold increased probability of achieving a viral cure — sustained undetectable HBV DNA off all therapy — compared with those with detectable pgRNA. Undetectable pgRNA is therefore not merely a mechanistic marker; it is the strongest available predictor that a treatment has opened the door to genuine, off-treatment cure. That distinction separates PBGENE-HBV from other hepatitis B candidates in development. Antisense oligonucleotides, RNA interference agents, and capsid assembly modulators can knock down circulating viral proteins, but they leave cccDNA largely untouched. The moment therapy stops, cccDNA resumes templating new virus. By aiming a molecular scissors directly at the DNA source, Precision is attempting something categorically different: permanent deletion. The Technology: ARCUS Gene Editing PBGENE-HBV is built on Precision's proprietary ARCUS® genome editing platform , which derives from a class of naturally occurring enzymes called homing endonucleases. The specific precursor is I-CreI, a protein found in the green alga Chlamydomonas reinhardtii that belongs to the LAGLIDADG enzyme family — one of the oldest and most studied groups of sequence-specific DNA cutters in nature. Precision's protein engineers substantially redesigned I-CreI to redirect its specificity from the alga's own genome toward a conserved sequence in the HBV polymerase gene, converting the natural homodimer into a single-chain monomeric protein that recognizes roughly 22 base pairs of target sequence. That architecture distinguishes ARCUS from CRISPR-Cas9 in several practically important ways. CRISPR requires two components — the Cas9 protein and a separate guide RNA strand — to be packaged and delivered together. ARCUS is a single protein: its DNA recognition is encoded in the protein's own binding interface rather than in a separate nucleic acid. The result is a smaller mRNA payload, no guide RNA to trigger innate immune RNA sensors, and a longer recognition sequence (22 bp vs. the ~20 bp of a CRISPR spacer plus PAM) that statistically reduces the probability of off-target cutting events. In PBGENE-HBV, an mRNA blueprint for the ARCUS nuclease is encapsulated in lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) formulated by Acuitas Therapeutics — the same Vancouver-based LNP specialist whose platform was used in the Pfizer/BioNTech COVID-19 mRNA vaccine — and infused intravenously into the patient. The LNPs home preferentially to hepatocytes via the ApoE-LDL receptor pathway, where the mRNA is translated into the nuclease protein. The protein finds and cuts the HBV cccDNA at its target sequence, triggering the cell's own repair machinery to introduce small insertions or deletions — indels — that disrupt the viral polymerase gene and permanently inactivate replication from that copy of cccDNA. Crucially, unlike AAV-based gene therapies — which cannot be re-administered because patients generate immune responses against the viral capsid — PBGENE-HBV can be infused multiple times. Patients in ELIMINATE-B received multiple infusions across the dosing period, and the cumulative editing effect appears to build as a larger fraction of hepatocytes is progressively reached and edited. The Clinical Data in Detail pgRNA Loss: Durable and Complete at the Go-Forward Dose Among the 12 evaluable patients with detectable pgRNA at baseline, 11 reached undetectable levels. The one exception — dosed at the higher 0.65 mg/kg level but with only two administrations — still showed a 95% reduction in pgRNA at the data cut and continues on follow-up. At 0.4 mg/kg, the response was total. All six evaluable patients at that dose reached undetectable pgRNA, and among the subset with at least six months of follow-up, every single one has maintained undetectable pgRNA through the September 28, 2026 cutoff. The longest-running observation now approaches one year of sustained pgRNA loss , extending data first presented at the European Association for the Study of the Liver (EASL) Congress in Barcelona in May 2026. A Hard-to-Treat Patient: The HBeAg-Positive Signal One particularly notable data point involves the first HBeAg-positive patient enrolled in ELIMINATE-B. HBeAg positivity is a marker of active, high-level viral replication and is generally considered the more difficult phenotype to treat: HBeAg-positive patients carry logarithmically higher amounts of cccDNA and viral load in liver tissue. At the 0.4 mg/kg dose, that patient's pgRNA dropped from 5,750 copies/mL at baseline to undetectable after just the second administration of PBGENE-HBV, and remained undetectable through the data cutoff. That the editing machinery could penetrate and clear cccDNA even under those conditions is likely to attract close attention from hepatologists accustomed to treating this population with less certainty of outcome. Liver Biopsies: Proving the Mechanism pgRNA is a blood-based surrogate. The more direct measurement is what happens in the liver itself, and Precision presented paired biopsy data — tissue samples taken before and after treatment — from two patients dosed at 0.4 mg/kg. The results showed approximately 10-fold (1-log) and 35-fold (1.5-log) reductions in cccDNA , respectively. These findings reproduce and extend the first-ever biopsy evidence of cccDNA elimination in treated patients, which Precision disclosed at EASL 2026. The convergence between vanishing cccDNA in liver tissue and vanishing pgRNA in blood provides what researchers characterize as mechanistic confirmation that the therapy is doing precisely what it was designed to do. It is worth stating the biological caveat precisely: a reduction in cccDNA is not identical to its complete elimination. What the biopsy data demonstrate is a measurable, large-magnitude reduction in cccDNA copies per cell — reductions that, when combined with sustained pgRNA undetectability, provide the strongest available human evidence that ARCUS editing is progressively clearing the reservoir. Whether residual cccDNA below current detection thresholds persists is a question Phase 2 will need to address. HBsAg: Broad Antiviral Activity Across the Board Hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) — a secondary viral marker produced from both cccDNA and from integrated HBV DNA embedded elsewhere in the genome — declined in all 24 evaluable patients across every dose level tested. At 0.4 mg/kg, maximal HBsAg reductions approached 94%. While HBsAg reductions alone are not sufficient to demonstrate cccDNA clearance (because integrated HBV DNA also contributes to surface antigen), the consistency of the effect across the entire evaluable population reinforces that PBGENE-HBV is producing broad antiviral activity in a diverse patient group. Safety: No Dose-Limiting Toxicities Across all 59 infusions in 25 patients, Precision reports no dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs). The most common adverse events were infusion-related reactions characteristic of lipid nanoparticle delivery — typically beginning and resolving within 24 hours of infusion. The 0.4 mg/kg dose was better tolerated than the higher 0.65 mg/kg dose, an observation that reinforces the Phase 2 dose selection. At 0.4 mg/kg, no serious adverse events (SAEs) have been recorded, and no Grade 3 or higher hypotension events have occurred across 15 administrations in eight patients. Transient, reversible elevations in liver enzymes (ALT and AST) were observed following some infusions, consistent with known LNP biology. One delayed elevation occurred after a third administration — peak ALT of 306 U/L, AST of 278 U/L — but resolved without intervention and without associated changes in bilirubin or any sign of synthetic liver dysfunction. No cases meeting Hy's Law criteria — a pharmacovigilance threshold for serious drug-induced liver injury — were observed at any dose level. Executive and Investigator Perspectives Michael Amoroso , President and CEO of Precision BioSciences, framed the data as a threshold moment: "The consistency of the findings at the 0.4 mg/kg dose, including complete and durable pgRNA loss to undetectable levels, reproducible liver biopsies demonstrating evidence of cccDNA elimination, and a treatment effect in all patients with a manageable safety profile, supports advancement to a global Phase 2 study." Dr. Cassie Gorsuch , Precision's Chief Scientific Officer, highlighted the HBeAg-positive result as the dataset's headline finding: "The highlight of the data was demonstrating pgRNA loss to undetectable levels in our first HBeAg positive patient dosed on study, indicating potent effect of PBGENE-HBV in a patient population that is very difficult to treat due to logarithmically higher amounts of cccDNA and viral load in the liver." Lead investigator Dr. Man-Fung Yuen connected the biopsy findings to the clinical ambition behind the program: "Reproducing the liver biopsy evidence of cccDNA elimination in a second patient treated at 0.4 mg/kg, together with durable pgRNA loss in patients with longer follow-up, is particularly important because it links the intended mechanism of action, cccDNA elimination, to ongoing antiviral activity. These findings provide a meaningful foundation for Phase 2 testing and for assessing whether directly targeting cccDNA can ultimately enable patients to stop long-term suppressive therapy and achieve cure." What Comes Next: ELIMINATE-B2 Based on the Phase 1 readout, Precision has formally selected 0.4 mg/kg as the go-forward dose and is advancing to ELIMINATE-B2 , a global Phase 2 study that will evaluate PBGENE-HBV both as a standalone treatment and in combination with an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) . The ASO combination arm is designed to address the integrated HBV DNA that is not targeted by PBGENE-HBV alone, with the goal of maximizing the probability of functional cure — a state in which patients can cease NUC therapy while maintaining undetectable HBV DNA. The FDA has previously indicated that sustained undetectable HBV DNA off NUC therapy is a validated endpoint for a curative label claim. PBGENE-HBV already holds FDA Fast Track designation for this indication. Precision expects to initiate dosing in ELIMINATE-B2 in Q1 2027 , with enrollment planned across multiple continents. Initial open-label data from Phase 2 are anticipated in the second half of 2027 . The company will host an investor and key opinion leader (KOL) webcast on Monday, November 9, 2026 at 8:00 a.m. ET to walk through the full AASLD dataset. Context: Why This Disease Has Been So Hard to Crack Chronic hepatitis B infects an estimated 254 million people globally and kills approximately 1.1 million people each year from cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma — making viral hepatitis the second-leading infectious cause of death worldwide, according to the WHO 2026 Global Hepatitis Report . Despite a preventive vaccine available since the early 1980s and highly effective antiviral drugs that have been available for decades, the number of people living with chronic infection has barely moved — in part because the vaccine cannot help those already infected, and in part because current antivirals cannot cure them. The cccDNA reservoir is the reason. Once a hepatocyte is infected, cccDNA forms in the nucleus as a stable, episomal (non-integrating, plasmid-like) minichromosome — a small circular piece of viral DNA wrapped in host cell proteins, maintained through rounds of cell division, invisible to the immune system, and not targeted by current antivirals. It can persist for the lifetime of an infected individual. Even when HBsAg seroconverts to undetectable — an event considered a functional cure in clinical practice today — cccDNA can remain dormant in the liver, capable of reactivating if immunity wanes. That reactivation risk is not hypothetical. Each year, an estimated 300,000 or more chronic HBV patients globally require immunosuppressive therapies — cancer chemotherapy, anti-rejection drugs after organ transplantation, biological therapies for autoimmune diseases. For these patients, residual cccDNA is a ticking clock; reactivation, when it occurs, can cause acute liver failure. A therapy that eliminates cccDNA would address this risk structurally, not just suppress its probability. That biological obstacle has made the PBGENE-HBV program one of the more closely watched in hepatology. No prior therapy had demonstrated direct, confirmed, repeat molecular editing of cccDNA in human liver tissue — until the biopsy data first shown at EASL and now expanded at AASLD. Competitive Landscape Precision is not alone in pursuing hepatitis B cure, but PBGENE-HBV occupies a distinct mechanistic category. GSK and Ionis's antisense oligonucleotide bepirovirsen completed Phase 3 trials (B-Well 1 & 2) targeting viral RNA and surface antigen, achieving a functional cure rate of 19% — a meaningful improvement over standard of care but still leaving the large majority of patients without durable cure, and without touching cccDNA. RNA interference agents from companies including Vir Biotechnology, Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, and Dicerna (now part of Novo Nordisk) have demonstrated potent suppression of circulating HBsAg. And a growing number of immunotherapeutic candidates — checkpoint inhibitors, T-cell engagers, therapeutic vaccines — aim to reinvigorate the immune response against the virus. What distinguishes PBGENE-HBV in this landscape is its direct attack on the cccDNA molecule itself, rather than on downstream products of cccDNA transcription. The theory is that eliminating the template could produce a more durable off-treatment response than any downstream suppression strategy — and could address the reactivation risk that even functional cure leaves unresolved. The Phase 2 data, expected in the second half of next year, will begin to test whether that theory translates into cure rates. The Bottom Line Precision BioSciences today presented the most detailed human evidence yet that in vivo gene editing can selectively reach, cut, and silence the hepatitis B cccDNA reservoir that has resisted every therapeutic approach for more than four decades. The Phase 1 signal — complete pgRNA loss in all evaluable patients at the go-forward dose, biopsy-confirmed cccDNA reductions reaching 35-fold, and a clean safety profile across nearly 60 infusions — is striking enough that the company is movi...

Bepirovirsen Functional Cure Durable Through Week 96 in Chronic Hepatitis B - HCPLive
新闻简报HCPLive 于 2026 年 11 月 7 日发表的新闻报道讨论了一项使用贝匹罗韦森对 慢性乙型肝炎 的 功能性治愈 进行的研究,该研究显示出持续第 96 周的持久反应。该研究侧重于患者报告的数据和 治疗 结果,没有提供具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数。这些发现基于临床试验的结果,并作为确认的结果呈现。该报告没有提及源数据中任何未解决的信息或限制。该分析以人类 乙型肝炎病毒 感染为中心,包括 慢性乙型肝炎 以及肝细胞癌和肝硬化等相关病症。证据不包括有关病毒传播、卫生系统影响或运营影响的详细信息。该研究是更广泛的乙型肝炎治疗方案的一部分,凸显了贝匹罗韦森在治疗该疾病方面的潜力。

Bepirovirsen Functional Cure Durable Through Week 96 in Chronic Hepatitis B - HCPLive

News Brief
The news report from HCPLive, published on November 7, 2026, discusses a study on the functional cure of chronic hepatitis B using bepirovirsen, showing durable response through week 96. The study focuses on patient-reported data and treatment outcomes, with no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers provided. The findings are based on results from clinical trials and are presented as confirmed outcomes. The report does not mention any unresolved information or limitations in the source data. The analysis is centered on human hepatitis B virus infection, including chronic hepatitis B and related conditions such as hepatocellular carcinoma and cirrhosis. The evidence does not include details about the spread of the virus, health-system effects, or operational impacts. The study is part of the broader therapeutic pipeline for hepatitis B, highlighting the potential of bepirovirsen in managing the disease.

Updates from AASLD 2026 in chronic hepatitis B - springermedicine.com

News Brief
The news report from Springer Medicine, published on November 9, 2026, provides updates from the AASLD 2026 conference on chronic hepatitis B and related conditions such as late-onset genetic cholestasis and Alagille syndrome. It highlights key studies and expert commentary, including discussions on the HARMONi-GI1 trial of ivonescimab plus chemotherapy in advanced biliary tract cancer. The report serves as a general educational resource and does not report any confirmed or suspected outbreak events. It focuses on medical research and clinical updates rather than specific public health responses or statistics. No case counts, death counts, or risk levels are mentioned, and the content is intended for medical professionals seeking trusted, cutting-edge education in their specialties.

乙型肝炎 疫苗 应答者和慢性患者的 T 细胞受体谱存在差异 - Bioengineer.org

T-Cell Receptor Profiles Differ in Hepatitis B Vaccine Responders and Chronic Patients - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 8 日发表的一项研究表明,与乙型肝炎 疫苗 应答者和健康捐赠者相比,慢性乙型肝炎 患者表现出明显更少的独特 T 细胞受体 α 链和 β 链互补决定区 3 序列。该研究发表在《BMC 免疫学》上,分析了六名 疫苗 应答者、六名 慢性乙型肝炎 患者和五名健康捐赠者的 T 细胞受体库。研究发现,慢性乙型肝炎 组的 α 链和 β 链序列多样性均降低,片段利用率存在显着差异。该研究强调了与 疫苗接种 反应性和慢性疾病状态相关的免疫差异,可能有助于了解保护性免疫并为未来乙型肝炎的诊断和预防策略提供信息。没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,也没有明确说明风险水平。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 11 月 8 日发表,研究结果基于从不同乙型肝炎感染和 疫苗接种 状态的个体收集的数据。
地点与对象
该研究涉及六名乙型肝炎 疫苗 反应者、六名 慢性乙型肝炎 患者和五名健康捐赠者,未指定特定地理位置。
事件
研究人员发现,与乙型肝炎 疫苗 应答者和健康捐赠者相比,慢性乙型肝炎 患者表现出明显更少的独特 T 细胞受体 α 链和 β 链互补决定区 3 序列。这项研究由 Bioengineer.org 报道。
规模、影响与风险
该研究确定了三组之间 T 细胞受体多样性的差异,其中 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的序列多样性降低。没有报告具体病例、死亡或暴露计数,也没有明确说明风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究小组利用高通量测序对各组的 T 细胞受体库进行了系统比较。这些发现基于已发表的研究,尚未进行调查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

T-Cell Receptor Profiles Differ in Hepatitis B Vaccine Responders and Chronic Patients - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 8, 2026, by Bioengineer.org reveals that individuals with chronic hepatitis B exhibit significantly fewer unique T-cell receptor alpha-chain and beta-chain complementarity-determining region 3 sequences compared to hepatitis B vaccine responders and healthy donors. The research, published in BMC Immunology, analyzed the T-cell receptor repertoires of six vaccine responders, six chronic hepatitis B patients, and five healthy donors. It found that the chronic hepatitis B group had reduced sequence diversity in both alpha and beta chains, with notable differences in segment utilization. The study highlights immune differences linked to vaccination responsiveness and chronic disease states, potentially aiding in understanding protective immunity and informing future diagnostic and preventive strategies for hepatitis B. No specific case or death counts were reported, and the risk level was not explicitly stated.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health T-Cell Receptor Profiles Differ in Hepatitis B Vaccine Responders and Chronic Patients by November 8, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 T-Cell Receptor Profiles Differ in Hepatitis B Vaccine Responders and Chronic Patients Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Individuals with chronic hepatitis B exhibit significantly fewer unique T-cell receptor alpha-chain and beta-chain complementarity-determining region 3 sequences than hepatitis B vaccine-positive responders and healthy control donors. According to research published in BMC Immunology, circulating T-cell receptor repertoires demonstrate distinct diversity, overlap, and segment usage patterns across these clinical cohorts. T cells play a vital role during hepatitis B virus infection by mediating both protective immunity and immunopathology. In-depth profiling of the complementarity-determining region 3 within the T-cell receptor provides critical insights into the composition and functional capacity of circulating T-cell populations. Prior to this investigation, the specific repertoire characteristics of the T-cell receptor alpha-chain and beta-chain across individuals with varying hepatitis B infection and vaccination statuses remained incompletely understood. To systematically evaluate these immune features, researchers employed high-throughput sequencing to profile the alpha-chain and beta-chain complementarity-determining region 3 repertoires within peripheral blood mononuclear cells. The study cohort comprised six inoculation-positive responders to hepatitis B vaccination, six individuals diagnosed with chronic hepatitis B, and five healthy donors. The research team systematically compared the frequency, diversity, sequence similarity, and variable and joining segment usage across the three examined groups. Discover more Study Biology Find Vaccines vaccine Vaccine Study Chemistry Sequence diversity comparisons demonstrated that the chronic hepatitis B group harbored significantly fewer unique alpha-chain and beta-chain complementarity-determining region 3 sequences than both the inoculation-positive responder group and the healthy donor group. Repertoire overlap analysis revealed marked differences in segment utilization among the study cohorts. The BUB overlap index for alpha-chain variable segments was significantly higher in inoculation-positive responders than in individuals with chronic hepatitis B (P = 0.0073) and healthy donors (P = 0.0317). For beta-chain variable segments, a significantly higher BUB index emerged specifically between inoculation-positive responders and chronic hepatitis B patients (P = 0.0002). Furthermore, identical alpha-chain complementarity-determining region 3 sequences were preferentially shared among individuals within each respective group, whereas beta-chain joining segment usage remained relatively stable across all three cohorts. The investigators concluded that circulating T cells display distinct alpha-beta complementarity-determining region 3 repertoire profiles in inoculation-positive responders and chronic hepatitis B patients. These observed signatures delineate immune differences linked to hepatitis B vaccination responsiveness and chronic disease state, which may help enhance understanding of protective vaccine-induced immunity and inform future investigations into alpha- and beta-chain strategies for hepatitis B virus diagnosis and prevention. 沉默 HBV DNA:治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 的新方法

Silencing HBV DNA: A New Approach to Treating Chronic Hepatitis B - מכון דוידסון לחינוך מדעי
新闻简报一种治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 的新实验技术已经开发出来,针对 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) DNA 来沉默其活性。这项发表在《自然生物医学工程》上的研究表明,小鼠体内的病毒标记物显着且持续减少。这种方法的目的是防止病毒产生必需的蛋白质和复制。治疗 已进入人体 1/2 期临床试验,首例患者于 2026 年 1 月接受该试验。然而,其在人体中的安全性和有效性的结果尚未公布。如果证明成功,该方法可以为慢性乙型肝炎提供一种新颖的 治疗 选择。该技术利用 DNA 甲基化(一种自然细胞过程)来抑制病毒基因表达。这可能会导致长期影响,而不是暂时抑制病毒。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 8 日(N7)。
地点与对象
全球(N11),特别提到了研究中的小鼠(N5)。
事件
开发了一种新的实验技术来沉默 HBV DNA,减少小鼠体内的病毒蛋白和遗传物质 (N5)。这种方法针对病毒基因以阻止其功能和繁殖(N14)。
规模、影响与风险
该研究表明小鼠(N15)的病毒标记物显着且持续减少。实验性 治疗 已进入人体 1/2 期临床试验,首例患者于 2026 年 1 月接受该药物(N16)。然而,尚无关于人类安全性或有效性的结果(N17)。
应对、状态与不确定性
实验性 治疗 正在与传统 抗病毒药物 疗法 (N37) 一起进行临床试验。该研究的结果表明了潜在的长期影响(N55)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Silencing HBV DNA: A New Approach to Treating Chronic Hepatitis B - מכון דוידסון לחינוך מדעי

News Brief
A new experimental technique to treat chronic hepatitis B has been developed, targeting the hepatitis B virus (HBV) DNA to silence its activity. The study, published in Nature Biomedical Engineering, demonstrated a significant and sustained reduction in viral markers in mice. This method aims to prevent the virus from producing essential proteins and replicating. The treatment has entered Phase 1/2 clinical trials in humans, with the first patient receiving it in January 2026. However, results on its safety and effectiveness in humans are not yet available. The approach could offer a novel treatment option for chronic HBV if proven successful. The technique leverages DNA methylation, a natural cellular process, to inhibit viral gene expression. This could potentially lead to long-lasting effects rather than temporary suppression of the virus.
Original source text (English)

Tal Fisher Tal Fisher share הקישור הועתק! 8 November 2026 | 6 minutes Getting your Trinity Audio player ready... Hepatitis B is a disease caused by the hepatitis B virus (HBV), which infects liver cells. Some people infected with the virus recover completely, while in others, the infection becomes chronic and can persist for many years. Chronic hepatitis B often causes no noticeable symptoms, but over time it can cause serious liver damage and lead to cirrhosis or even liver cancer. The disease is widespread: according to the World Health Organization, approximately 240 million people worldwide currently live with chronic hepatitis B. Existing antiviral treatments can suppress viral replication and reduce the risk of liver damage and serious complications, but they generally cannot eliminate the virus completely or fully stop the production of viral proteins. In a recent study, published in the journal Nature Biomedical Engineering, researchers investigated a novel approach to treating chronic hepatitis B. They used a technique designed to silence viral genes, preventing them from producing the proteins the virus needs to function and reproduce. The results were promising: mice carrying HBV and treated with the new method showed a substantial and sustained reduction in viral markers. The experimental treatment has also entered Phase 1/2 clinical trials in humans, with the first patient receiving it in January 2026. However, results establishing its safety or effectiveness in humans are not yet available. If proven safe and effective, the approach could eventually offer a new treatment option for people living with chronic hepatitis B. HBV infects liver cells and delivers its genetic material into their nuclei. Illustration of the liver and the hepatitis B virus | KATERYNA KON / Science Photo Library A Virus That Won’t Leave Viruses are tiny infectious agents that contain genetic material enclosed in a protein coat and cannot reproduce on their own. Once inside a host cell, they hijack its machinery to produce viral proteins and replicate their genetic material, ultimately generating new viruses. Viruses differ in several ways, including the type of genetic material they carry: some have DNA, while others have RNA. In our cells, DNA serves as a kind of recipe book, containing instructions for producing RNA molecules. Some of these RNA molecules then serve as templates for protein production.DNA is stored in the cell nucleus, while RNA molecules carrying instructions for protein production leave the nucleus and travel to ribosomes, where they serve as templates for synthesizing proteins. Viruses also rely on these basic processes, although where their genetic material is found and how it is copied and expressed vary from one virus to another. HBV is a DNA virus that infects liver cells and delivers its genetic material into their nuclei. There, the viral DNA is converted into a stable, circular form that remains in the nucleus and serves as a template for producing viral RNA. The cell then uses this RNA to manufacture the viral proteins needed to produce new viruses. During infection, some viral DNA may also become integrated into the DNA of the host cell. Once integrated, the viral sequences are copied along with the cell’s own DNA whenever it divides, making them difficult to eliminate without destroying the cell. Although this integrated DNA generally cannot produce complete, infectious HBV particles, it can continue to serve as a source of viral RNA and proteins. As a result, even when antiviral treatments suppress viral replication, infected cells may continue producing certain viral proteins. Existing and New Treatments Chronic HBV infection is commonly treated with drugs called nucleos(t)ide analogues, or NUCs. These drugs interfere with viral DNA replication, suppress the production of new viruses, and reduce the risk of liver disease progression. However, they generally do not eliminate the circular viral DNA that persists in the nuclei of infected cells or completely stop the production of viral proteins. As a result, treatment with NUCs is usually long-term and generally does not eliminate the infection. In the ongoing clinical trial, the experimental treatment is being administered alongside conventional antiviral therapy. In the new study, researchers investigated a different approach that takes advantage of a natural cellular process called DNA methylation. This process involves attaching small chemical groups, known as methyl groups, to DNA, which can affect gene activity. Methylation of certain regions of HBV DNA can suppress viral gene expression and reduce the production of viral RNA. To harness this mechanism, the researchers developed an experimental treatment called CRMA-1001. The treatment uses CRISPR technology, which allows scientists to target specific DNA sequences with high precision. While CRISPR is commonly used to cut DNA, in this case the researchers combined it with a system that adds methyl groups to specific regions of HBV DNA, suppressing viral gene expression without cutting the DNA itself. In the new study, researchers propose a different treatment approach that takes advantage of a naturally occurring cellular process called DNA methylation. During methylation, chemical groups known as methyl groups are added to DNA, affecting how it functions. In certain regions, methylation of HBV DNA can suppress its activity and reduce RNA production. To harness this phenomenon, the researchers developed an experimental treatment called CRMA-1001. The treatment is based on CRISPR technology, which enables precise targeting of specific sites in DNA. CRISPR is typically used to cut DNA, but in this case, it was coupled with a system designed to methylate regions of HBV DNA, with the aim of suppressing viral activity. In the study, researchers substantially reduced the amount of viral DNA circulating in the blood of treated mice. A blood test measuring HBV viral load | bangoland / Shutterstock Silencing the Virus The researchers tested the treatment in mice using several experimental approaches. In one experiment, in which viral DNA was integrated into the mice’s own DNA, a single dose of the treatment substantially reduced viral levels, an effect that persisted for seven months. In another experiment, which examined viral DNA that remained separate from the host cells’ DNA, three doses administered approximately one month apart produced even more striking results. Six months after treatment began, viral levels in approximately 90 percent of the mice that received a high dose had fallen below the level that the test could reliably measure. These findings suggest that the treatment may have long-lasting effects rather than merely temporarily suppressing viral activity. Following the experiments in mice, and before beginning clinical trials in humans, the researchers evaluated the treatment’s safety in monkeys, whose physiology is more similar to that of humans. Overall, the treatment did not appear to cause unintended changes in gene activity or interfere with the function of the monkeys’ own DNA. However, a temporary increase in liver enzyme levels raised questions that will require further investigation before the treatment can be tested more widely in humans. The study presents a promising new approach to treating chronic hepatitis B. It is important to remember, however, that a safe and effective vaccine is already available to prevent HBV infection. The vaccine cannot treat an infection that has already occurred, and this is where the new approach could make a difference: it is designed to help people who are already living with chronic hepatitis B. If clinical trials establish that the treatment is safe and effective in humans, it could eventually offer a new option for managing chronic hepatitis B. Although the research is still at an early stage, the findings are encouraging. The study illustrates how a deeper understanding of fundamental molecular processes can potentially lead to development of innovative medical treatments. By combining knowledge of how HBV operates within infected cells with advances in molecular technology, the researchers developed a way to suppress viral activity by silencing its DNA, rather than simply blocking viral replication, as existing treatments do. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B Functional Cure Persists Off Treatment After Bepirovirsen - The American Journal of Managed Care

News Brief
A functional cure for chronic hepatitis B was maintained in 90% to 92% of patients 48 weeks after stopping all therapy, according to data presented at The Liver Meeting 2026. The most common adverse events were upper respiratory tract infection, decreased creatinine renal clearance, and nasopharyngitis, occurring in 4%, 2%, and 2% of patients, respectively. The findings are based on phase 3 B-Well 1 and 2 trials and are model-based predictions for patients with baseline HBsAg below 100 IU/mL.
Original source text (English)
Hepatitis B functional cure with bepirovirsen held in 90% to 92% of responders 48 weeks after stopping all therapy, per data at The Liver Meeting 2026. A hepatitis B functional cure achieved with 24 weeks of bepirovirsen (Hibsago; GSK) was maintained by about 9 in 10 responders through 48 weeks after stopping all hepatitis B virus (HBV) treatment, according to end-of-study results from the phase 3 B-Well 1 and 2 trials presented at The Liver Meeting 2026 . 1 Two other presentations at the meeting modeled response in patients with lower antigen levels and laid out a US care pathway for delivering finite therapy. 2,3 Among patients who achieved functional cure, stopped nucleos(t)ide analogues, and completed follow-up, 90% and 92% maintained the response at week 96, 48 weeks after all HBV treatment ended. | Image credit: Vitalii Vodolazskyi - stock.adobe.com The data arrive weeks after the FDA approved bepirovirsen on October 20, 2026, as the first antisense oligonucleotide for chronic HBV infection. 4 It is indicated in combination with nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA) therapy for adults without cirrhosis who have received NA therapy for at least 6 months, have suppressed HBV DNA, and have hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels of 3000 IU/mL or less as determined by an FDA-authorized test. 4 About 1.7 million people in the United States live with chronic HBV, according to GSK. 4 Standard NA therapy suppresses the virus but is typically taken indefinitely. Functional cure, defined in the B-Well trials as at least 24 weeks of sustained undetectable HBV DNA and HBsAg loss after finite therapy, is described by the investigators as the treatment goal in chronic HBV. 1 “Current care pathways for chronic hepatitis B (CHB) relying on long-term viral suppression deliver minimal functional cure (FC) rates,” wrote the authors of the US care pathway. 3 B-Well 1 ( NCT05630807 ) and B-Well 2 ( NCT05630820 ) were global, double-blind trials in adults without cirrhosis whose HBV was suppressed on NA therapy and whose HBsAg was above 100 IU/mL and at or below 3000 IU/mL. 1 Patients were randomly assigned 2:1 to bepirovirsen 300 mg or placebo weekly for 24 weeks, and eligible patients discontinued NAs at week 48. The primary outcome was functional cure at week 72. The pivotal results were previously presented at the European Association for the Study of the Liver Congress 2026 and published in the New England Journal of Medicine . 5 At week 72, 20% (127 of 650) of patients receiving bepirovirsen in B-Well 1 and 19% (106 of 570) in B-Well 2 achieved functional cure, compared with no patients receiving placebo. 1 About 24% of patients receiving bepirovirsen entered the durability stage after week 72. Among patients who stopped NAs, achieved functional cure at week 72, and completed week 96, 90% (114 of 127) in B-Well 1 and 92% (97 of 105) in B-Well 2 had a durable response, and similar durability was seen among those with baseline HBsAg of 1000 IU/mL or less. One patient who stopped NAs had a viral relapse and restarted NA therapy after week 72. Sign in or create an account to see articles, videos, and podcasts matched to your specialties. From weeks 73 to 96, the most common adverse events (AEs) were upper respiratory tract infection (4%), decreased creatinine renal clearance (2%), and nasopharyngitis (2%). Treatment-related AEs in that period occurred in 2% of patients and were single events. The investigators said bepirovirsen “had an acceptable safety profile without any evidence of latent safety concerns associated with prior [bepirovirsen] treatment.” Because the B-Well trials did not enroll patients with HBsAg below 100 IU/mL, GSK investigators used model-based extrapolation from 1194 B-Well participants benchmarked against 2067 patients in the real-world Everest study of pegylated interferon to estimate response in that group. 2 Functional cure rates in B-Well rose as baseline HBsAg fell, reaching 40% in the 100 to 200 IU/mL subgroup. The final model predicted mean functional cure rates of approximately 46% to 53% with 24 weeks of bepirovirsen in NA-treated patients with baseline HBsAg below 100 IU/mL, with the lower bound of the model’s 95% CI suggesting at least a 34% rate of functional cure in this patient population. Notably, the low-HBsAg estimates are model predictions, not trial results, and were produced by GSK, the drug’s manufacturer. A multidisciplinary panel of US clinicians, pharmacists, and advocates used a nominal group technique across 2 consensus meetings in 2026 to adapt a 9-step global care pathway proposed in 2025. 3 The first meeting, mainly of nonspecialists, produced a 6-step pathway, and the second, mainly of specialists, expanded it to 8 steps, each with 100% consensus. The final pathway separates shared decision-making at the start and end of treatment and adds detail to monitoring. Panelists identified quantitative HBsAg testing, access to functional cure treatment, patient engagement and adherence tools, provider education, care coordinators, and community awareness as priorities for implementation. “The new 8-step US FC pathway and implementation priorities may help guide the paradigm shift in CHB management from chronic viral suppression to finite FC therapy,” the authors concluded. 3 1. FDA 批准首个 慢性乙型肝炎 治疗 - 医学对话

FDA Approves First-of-Its-Kind Chronic Hepatitis B Treatment - Medical Dialogues
新闻简报2026年11月7日,美国FDA批准Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)注射液与现有的抗病毒药物疗法联合用于治疗无肝硬化成人慢性乙型肝炎。这标志着反义寡核苷酸首次获得批准用于治疗这种疾病。治疗 专为接受 抗病毒药物 治疗至少六个月且 HBV 水平和 HBsAg 水平抑制≤3,000 IU/mL 的患者而设计。临床试验表明,接受 Hibsago 治疗的患者中有 24% 符合停止现有药物的标准,而接受安慰剂治疗的患者则没有。治疗 治疗 24 周后,接受 Hibsago 治疗的患者中有 19% 的病毒仍得到控制,血液中检测不到 HBsAg。FDA 已针对严重肝脏反应、血小板计数低和其他风险发出黑框警告,要求定期进行血液检查并与医疗保健提供者进行随访。该批准已授予 GSK plc,包括快速通道、突破性疗法和优先审评资格。这代表了管理 慢性乙型肝炎 的重要进步。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The US FDA approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) injection on 2026-11-07.
地点与对象
美国;接受 HBV 抗病毒药物 治疗至少六个月的无肝硬化成人。
事件
FDA 批准 Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)注射液与现有的 抗病毒药物 疗法联合用于治疗某些无肝硬化成人的 慢性乙型肝炎。
规模、影响与风险
此次批准为选定的 慢性乙型肝炎 患者引入了一种基于反义的新型 治疗 方法,补充了现有的 抗病毒药物 疗法。治疗 对严重肝脏反应、血小板计数低、肾功能恶化以及血管炎等严重炎症不良反应进行了黑框警告。患者需要定期进行血液检查并与医疗保健提供者进行随访以监测这些风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 授予 Hibsago 快速通道和突破性疗法称号以及优先审查资格。葛兰素史克公司获得了批准。治疗 被授权作为伴随诊断设备,帮助选择接受 治疗 治疗的患者,并在整个 治疗 治疗过程中协助监测患者。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA Approves First-of-Its-Kind Chronic Hepatitis B Treatment - Medical Dialogues

News Brief
On November 7, 2026, the US FDA approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) injection for treating chronic hepatitis B in adults without cirrhosis, in combination with existing antiviral therapy. This marks the first approval of an antisense oligonucleotide for this condition. The treatment is designed for patients who have received antiviral therapy for at least six months and have suppressed HBV levels and HBsAg levels of ≤3,000 IU/mL. Clinical trials showed that 24% of patients who received Hibsago met the criteria to stop their existing medicine, compared to none who received a placebo. After 24 weeks of treatment, 19% of patients who received Hibsago still had the virus under control, with HBsAg undetectable in their blood. The FDA has issued boxed warnings for serious liver reactions, low platelet counts, and other risks, requiring regular blood tests and follow-up with healthcare providers. The approval was granted to GSK plc and includes Fast Track, Breakthrough Therapy, and Priority Review designations. This represents an important advance in managing chronic hepatitis B.
Original source text (English)
FDA Approves First-of-Its-Kind Chronic Hepatitis B Treatment The US FDA has approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) injection, in combination with existing antiviral therapy, for treating chronic hepatitis B in certain adults without cirrhosis. First-in-class: Hibsago is the first FDA-approved antisense oligonucleotide for chronic hepatitis B. Eligible patients: Adults without cirrhosis who have received HBV antiviral therapy for at least six months.Patients must have suppressed HBV levels and hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels of ≤3,000 IU/mL, confirmed by an FDA-authorised test.This approval introduces a novel antisense-based treatment approach for selected patients with chronic hepatitis B, complementing existing antiviral therapy. “For many people living with chronic hepatitis B, treatment can mean taking antiviral medication for years to keep the virus suppressed,” said Wendy Carter, DO, Acting Director of the Office of Infectious Diseases in the Center for Drug Evaluation and Research. “Hibsago provides a new treatment approach that gives some patients the possibility of maintaining control of the virus after all chronic HBV treatment has ended. This approval represents an important advance for people living with this serious disease.” Chronic hepatitis B is a long-term liver infection caused by HBV. Over time, the infection can lead to serious liver disease, including cirrhosis, and increase the risk of liver cancer. Hibsago works by targeting the genetic instructions the virus uses to make copies of itself and viral proteins, helping reduce the amount of virus and hepatitis B surface antigen in the body. The FDA also authorized the Roche Elecsys HBsAg II quant II RxDx assay as a companion diagnostic device to aid in the selection of patients with chronic HBV infection for treatment with antiviral therapy in accordance with the FDA approved drug labeling and to assist in monitoring patients throughout the course of treatment. Researchers studied Hibsago in two clinical trials that compared it with a placebo, an inactive treatment. Hibsago is injected under the skin for up to 24 weeks. Patients take it alongside their existing hepatitis B medicine and continue that medicine for at least 24 weeks after their last Hibsago injection. Some patients may then be able to stop their existing medicine if they meet certain health and lab test requirements. Across the two trials, 24% of patients who received Hibsago met the requirements to stop their existing medicine, compared with none who received placebo. Twenty-four weeks after stopping all hepatitis B treatment, 19% of patients who received Hibsago still had the virus under control, and a protein that signals hepatitis B infection (HBsAg) could not be detected in their blood. Hibsago has a boxed warning for serious liver reactions as well as low platelet counts, which can increase the risk of potentially life-threatening bleeding. Hibsago also has warnings for worsening kidney function, serious inflammatory adverse reactions like vasculitis, and serious risks if the injection is not administered properly. Patients need regular blood tests and follow up with their healthcare provider before and during treatment to monitor these risks. The most common side effects, excluding laboratory abnormalities, are injection site reactions, fever, fatigue and headache. The FDA granted Hibsago Fast Track and Breakthrough Therapy designations as well as Priority Review. The approval was granted to GSK plc. It also examines the extent to which LGBTQ+ health is addressed in medical education and identifies areas where additional training, resources, and clinical guidance may be beneficial. Who can participate? Medical doctors across specialties and levels of professional experience. Estimated duration: 8–10 minutes. Your participation will help identify educational gaps and inform future discussions on inclusive, respectful, and evidence-based healthcare. 美国肝移植受者中乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 发病率不理想

Suboptimal Hepatitis B Vaccination Rates in US Liver Transplant Recipients
新闻简报2026 年 9 月 29 日发表在《临床传染病》上的一项研究发现,乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 率不理想在美国肝移植受者中很常见。研究人员利用 2022 年 9 月至 2024 年 6 月的数据,分析了 13,851 名接受已故捐赠者肝脏移植的成年人。总体而言,根据自我报告的乙型肝炎 疫苗 (43.7%) 和/或抗 HB 抗体检测呈阳性 (38.6%),59.9% 的患者被认为已接种疫苗。接种疫苗的患者更有可能是女性、受教育程度较高且在移植等候名单上停留超过 6 个月。与未接种疫苗的患者相比,他们的丙型肝炎病毒感染和肝细胞癌的发生率也更高。研究指出,UNOS 地区的 疫苗接种 比率有所不同,东北部和西北部的比率较高,东南部的比率较低。研究人员强调需要采取多层次干预措施并使用更具免疫原性的 疫苗 来解决差异,特别是在老年人、酒精相关性肝病患者和 MELD-Na 评分较高的患者中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究使用2022年9月至2024年6月的数据进行,结果于2026年9月29日在线发布。
地点与对象
该分析包括 13,851 名在美国接受过已故捐赠者肝脏移植的成年人。
事件
一项回顾性 队列研究 研究发现,在美国肝移植受者中观察到乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 率不理想,其中 59.9% 的人根据自我报告的 HBV 疫苗 接种情况和/或抗 HB 抗体检测呈阳性,认为已接种疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
该研究发现,疫苗接种 发生率存在差异,年龄较大、酒精相关的肝病以及受教育程度较低与 疫苗接种 可能性降低相关。接种疫苗的患者中位年龄和 MELD-Na 评分较低,丙型肝炎病毒感染率和肝细胞癌发生率较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究强调需要采取有针对性的干预措施来提高 疫苗接种 率,特别是在老年人、ALD 患者和 MELD-Na 评分较高的患者中。它还注意到 疫苗接种 比率的地区和教育差异。

Suboptimal Hepatitis B Vaccination Rates in US Liver Transplant Recipients

News Brief
A study published in Clinical Infectious Diseases on September 29, 2026, found that suboptimal hepatitis B vaccination rates are common among liver transplant recipients in the United States. Using data from September 2022 to June 2024, researchers analyzed 13,851 adults who received liver transplants from deceased donors. Overall, 59.9% of patients were considered vaccinated based on self-reported receipt of the hepatitis B vaccine (43.7%) and/or a positive anti-HB antibody test (38.6%). Vaccinated patients were more likely to be women, have higher educational attainment, and spend more than 6 months on the transplant waitlist. They also had higher rates of hepatitis C virus infection and hepatocellular carcinoma compared to unvaccinated patients. The study noted that vaccination rates varied across UNOS regions, with higher rates in the Northeast and Northwest and lower rates in the Southeast. Researchers emphasized the need for multilevel interventions and the use of more immunogenic vaccines to address disparities, particularly among older adults, patients with alcohol-associated liver disease, and those with higher MELD-Na scores.

In the United States, a substantial proportion of liver transplant recipients do not receive hepatitis B vaccination, according to study findings published in Clinical Infectious Diseases. Vaccination against hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection is a key part of preventing adverse outcomes in patients with all types of chronic liver disease, including those undergoing transplantation, and is recommended in clinical guidelines for those being considered for a new organ. Researchers conducted a retrospective cohort study to examine HBV vaccine coverage using data from the United Network for Organ Sharing (UNOS), which requires all liver transplant programs to report HBV serologies and vaccination history for transplant recipients before the procedure. The analysis included 13,851 adults who received a liver from a deceased donor between September 2022 and June 2024. Multilevel interventions and the utilization of a more immunogenic vaccine may improve vaccination rates in this population. Overall, 59.9% of patients were considered vaccinated based on self-reported receipt of HBV vaccine (43.7%) and/or a positive anti-HB antibody test (38.6%). Compared with unvaccinated patients, vaccinated patients were more likely to be women (39.2% vs 36.2%), to have at least a high school education (91.7% vs 84.9%), and to have spent more than 6 months on the transplant waitlist (24.5% vs 20.1%). They also had higher rates of hepatitis C virus infection (3.8% vs 3.1%) and hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC; 19.9% vs 18.1%). Vaccinated patients also had a lower median age (56 vs 57 years) and median model for end-stage liver disease-sodium (MELD-Na) score at the time of UNOS listing (21 vs 23). In multivariate logistic regression analyses, positive predictors of HBV vaccination were as follows: Having a bachelor’s degree or higher (adjusted odds ratio [aOR], 1.09; 95% CI, 1.06-1.12); Black race (aOR, 1.08; 95% CI, 1.02-1.14); HCC diagnosis (aOR, 1.06; 95% CI, 1.02-1.10); Waitlist time of more than 6 months (aOR, 1.06; 95% CI, 1.02-1.11); and, Female sex (aOR, 1.05; 95% CI, 1.02-1.08). Conversely, older patients and those with alcohol-associated liver disease (ALD), other liver etiologies, a MELD-Na score of 30 or greater, and less than a high school education were less likely to be vaccinated. Vaccination rates varied across UNOS regions, tending to be higher in the Northeast and Northwest and lower in the Southeast. Study limitations include the lack of information on anti-HB antibody titer levels and potential confounders such as income and immigration status; the inability to assess factors such as variability in provider recommendations, vaccine hesitancy, and access to vaccination; and missing data for nontransplanted waitlist candidates. According to the researchers, “Multilevel interventions and the utilization of a more immunogenic vaccine may improve vaccination rates in this population.” They added, “Target interventions should focus on older adults, patients with ALD, and higher MELD-Na, while also addressing regional and educational disparities.” References: Shi MA, Han J, Albahra R, et al. Hepatitis B vaccination for liver transplant recipients in the United States: a national cohort study. Clin Infect Dis. Published online September 29, 2026. doi:10.1093/cid/ciag585

不同的蛋白质组模式将乙型肝炎 治疗 与 HBsAg 下降联系起来 - Bioengineer.org

Distinct proteomic patterns link hepatitis B treatments to HBsAg decline - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 10 日发表在《Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology》上的一项研究确定了与乙型肝炎 治疗 相关的不同纵向血浆蛋白质组模式,特别是 PD-1 阻断和 PEG-IFNα。该研究涉及 25 名已经接受核苷(酸)类似物治疗的 慢性乙型肝炎 患者。在三个时间点收集血浆样本:基线、第 12 周和第 24 周。研究发现,与 PD-1 阻断相比,PEG-IFNα 诱导更广泛的蛋白质组变化,IL-18R1、TNF、CX3CL1、CXCL9 和 CXCL10 等炎症标志物发生显着变化。然而,在错误发现率校正后,这些关联被认为是探索性的而非确定性的生物标志物。研究结果表明,跟踪一段时间内的动态免疫变化对于理解 慢性乙型肝炎 中的 治疗 反应可能很有价值。该研究的局限性包括样本量小,PD-1 阻断组有 10 名患者,PEG-IFNα 组有 15 名患者。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究进行了 24 周,在基线、第 12 周和第 24 周收集了血浆样本。发布日期为 2026 年 11 月 10 日。
地点与对象
该研究涉及 25 名 慢性乙型肝炎 患者,他们正在接受 PD-1 阻断或 PEG-IFNα 治疗。所有参与者均受到核苷(酸)类似物抑制。
事件
研究人员发现了与 慢性乙型肝炎 的两种免疫调节疗法相关的不同纵向血浆蛋白质组轨迹,表明特定的蛋白质变化可能与有利的 治疗 反应相关。该研究结果发表在《细胞和感染微生物学前沿》上的一项研究中。
规模、影响与风险
该研究分析了 25 名患者,其中 10 名患者属于 PD-1 阻断组,15 名患者属于 PEG-IFNα 组。研究发现,与 PD-1 阻断相比,PEG-IFNα 诱导更广泛的蛋白质组重塑,并且几种炎症标志物发生显着变化。然而,由于错误的发现率校正,这些关联被归类为探索性而非确定性生物标志物。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究正在进行中,研究结果被归类为探索性的。局限性包括样本量较小。该研究强调了纵向蛋白质组学对于理解乙型肝炎功能治疗策略的潜在价值。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Distinct proteomic patterns link hepatitis B treatments to HBsAg decline - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published in Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology on November 10, 2026, identified distinct longitudinal plasma proteomic patterns linked to hepatitis B treatments, specifically PD-1 blockade and PEG-IFNα. The research involved 25 patients with chronic hepatitis B who were already on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy. Plasma samples were collected at three time points: baseline, week 12, and week 24. The study found that PEG-IFNα induced broader proteomic changes compared to PD-1 blockade, with significant shifts in inflammatory markers like IL-18R1, TNF, CX3CL1, CXCL9, and CXCL10. However, these associations were deemed exploratory and not definitive biomarkers after false discovery rate correction. The findings suggest that tracking dynamic immune changes over time could be valuable for understanding treatment responses in chronic hepatitis B. The study's limitations include a small sample size, with ten patients in the PD-1 blockade group and fifteen in the PEG-IFNα group.
