HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Distinct proteomic patterns link hepatitis B treatments to HBsAg decline by November 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Distinct proteomic patterns link hepatitis B treatments to HBsAg decline Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers have identified distinct longitudinal plasma proteomic trajectories associated with two different immunomodulatory therapies for chronic hepatitis B, suggesting that specific protein changes may correlate with favorable treatment responses. The study, published in Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology, examined how pegylated interferon-alpha (PEG-IFNα) and PD-1 blockade alter the circulating inflammatory proteome in patients whose viral replication is already suppressed by nucleos(t)ide analogues. By profiling plasma samples over a 24-week period, the team found that while both therapies induce immune remodeling, the breadth and timing of these changes differ significantly between the two treatment modalities. The investigation focused on 25 patients with chronic hepatitis B who were receiving either PD-1 blockade or PEG-IFNα therapy. The cohort was divided into two groups: ten patients received PD-1 blockade, and fifteen patients received PEG-IFNα. All participants were nucleos(t)ide analogue-suppressed. Plasma samples were collected at three distinct time points: baseline, week 12, and week 24, providing a longitudinal view of how the proteome evolved in response to each therapy. The researchers performed longitudinal plasma proteomic profiling using the Olink Target 96 Inflammation panel. Of the proteins measured, seventy-seven passed the quality-control criteria and were included in the final analysis. The study employed linear mixed-effects models to analyze longitudinal protein changes. The primary results indicated that PEG-IFNα induced substantially broader longitudinal proteomic remodeling compared to PD-1 blockade. Most of the significant protein changes in the PEG-IFNα group occurred within the first 12 weeks of treatment. In contrast, the PD-1 blockade group showed a more limited range of proteomic shifts. Treatment-by-time analyses revealed distinct trajectories for the two therapies, highlighting differential changes in several key inflammatory markers, including IL-18R1, TNF, CX3CL1, CXCL9, and CXCL10. The researchers also evaluated the association between these proteomic changes and clinical response, defined as a decline in hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) of at least one log10 IU/mL by week 24. Using responder–non-responder comparisons and Spearman’s rank correlation, the team identified treatment-specific patterns linked to this favorable decline. In the PD-1 blockade cohort, IL-10 emerged as the leading exploratory signal associated with HBsAg decline. In the PEG-IFNα cohort, IL-12B, CDCP1, and FGF-21 were more prominent in the response-associated analyses. However, the study emphasizes that these response-related associations did not remain significant after false discovery rate correction. Consequently, the authors classified these findings as exploratory rather than definitive biomarkers. The findings support the potential value of longitudinal proteomics for mechanistic and biomarker studies of HBV functional-cure strategies. By demonstrating that favorable HBsAg decline is accompanied by treatment-specific rather than universal proteomic signatures, the research highlights the potential value of tracking dynamic immune changes over time. Limitations of the study include the relatively small sample size, with only ten patients in the PD-1 blockade group and fifteen in the PEG-IFNα group. HIV-HBV 混合感染与加蓬独特的生化特征有关 - Bioengineer.org

HIV-HBV Coinfection Linked to Distinct Biochemical Profile in Gabon - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2025 年 8 月 1 日至 11 月 30 日在加蓬利伯维尔进行的一项研究确定了同时感染 HIV 和 HBV 的患者具有独特的生化特征。该研究发表在 Bioengineer.org 的《Discover Medicine》杂志上,发现共同感染者的碱性磷酸酶、γ-谷氨酰转移酶、直接胆红素和总胆红素、肌酐和尿素水平升高,而总胆固醇显着降低。这些结果表明胆汁淤积和可能的肾功能障碍,这可能与双重感染的协同作用有关。该研究排除了接受抗逆转录病毒治疗的患者,并指出转氨酶水平在正常范围内,这可能是由于艾滋病毒引起的免疫抑制。研究人员强调了监测合并感染患者的这些标记物以控制潜在的健康恶化的重要性。该调查结果基于特定的 队列研究,并不表明公共卫生 暴发。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2025年8月1日至11月30日进行,新闻报道于2026年11月10日发布。
地点与对象
该研究在加蓬利伯维尔进行,涉及同时感染 HIV 和 HBV 的患者,其中包括 16 岁及以上的男性和女性。
事件
一项前瞻性横断面研究确定了同时感染 HIV 和 HBV 的患者具有独特的生化特征,该研究发表在 Bioengineer.org 的《Discover Medicine》杂志上。
规模、影响与风险
560 名 HIV 阳性患者中,60 人同时感染 HBV(10.71%)。共同感染组的碱性磷酸酶、γ-谷氨酰转移酶、直接胆红素和总胆红素、肌酐和尿素水平升高,而总胆固醇显着降低。这些发现表明存在胆汁淤积特征和潜在的肾功能障碍,对临床治疗具有影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究在中加合作医院进行,采用分光光度法分析生化参数。研究人员建议监测合并感染患者的这些标志物,并相应调整抗逆转录病毒治疗。该调查结果基于特定的 队列研究,并非公共卫生 暴发。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HIV-HBV Coinfection Linked to Distinct Biochemical Profile in Gabon - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted in Libreville, Gabon, from August 1 to November 30, 2025, identified a distinct biochemical profile in patients co-infected with HIV and HBV. Published in Discover Medicine by Bioengineer.org, the research found that co-infected individuals had elevated levels of alkaline phosphatase, gamma-glutamyl transferase, direct and total bilirubin, creatinine, and urea, while total cholesterol was significantly lower. These results suggest a cholestatic profile and possible renal dysfunction, which may be linked to the synergistic effects of the dual infection. The study excluded patients on antiretroviral therapy and noted that transaminase levels were within normal ranges, possibly due to HIV-induced immunosuppression. The researchers emphasized the importance of monitoring these markers in co-infected patients to manage potential health deterioration. The findings are based on a specific cohort and do not indicate a public health outbreak.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health HIV-HBV Coinfection Linked to Distinct Biochemical Profile in Gabon by November 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 HIV-HBV Coinfection Linked to Distinct Biochemical Profile in Gabon Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A prospective, cross-sectional study conducted in Libreville, Gabon, has identified a distinct biochemical profile among patients co-infected with the Human Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV) and the Hepatitis B Virus (HBV). The research, published in Discover Medicine, indicates that this dual infection is associated with specific abnormalities in liver and kidney function markers, differentiating these patients from those infected with HIV alone. The study aimed to determine the biochemical characteristics of this co-infected population to better understand the physiological impact of the combined viral infections on overall patient health. The study was carried out at the Medical Analysis Laboratory of the Sino-Gabonese Cooperation Hospital in Libreville from August 1 to November 30, 2025. It included men and women aged 16 years and older who presented for serological testing for HIV and hepatitis B, along with a biochemical profile. Patients who were already on antiretroviral therapy were excluded from the analysis, as such treatment can significantly affect lipid levels, liver enzymes, and renal markers. The study population consisted of individuals of various nationalities, with some being sexually active and others not. Discover more healthy Get Health Tips health Out of 560 patients who tested positive for HIV-1 and HIV-2 antibodies, 60 were found to be positive for the hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg), representing a prevalence of 10.71% within this specific cohort. The co-infected group was predominantly female, with 40 patients (66.67%) being women and 20 patients (33.33%) being men. The mean age of the co-infected patients was 36 years, with the most represented age group being 17 to 33 years. Socio-professionally, entrepreneurs constituted the largest group, followed by traders. Discover more Check Symptoms Get Tested infections infection The researchers analyzed several biochemical parameters, including glucose, uric acid, creatinine, urea, cholesterol, triglycerides, transaminases, gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT), and alkaline phosphatase (ALP). Blood samples were collected in dry tubes and centrifuged to obtain serum for analysis. The screening for HBsAg was performed using immunochromatographic tests and confirmed with the MINI VIDAS® automated assay. Biochemical parameters were determined using spectrophotometric methods on a SELECTRA PROS analyzer, ensuring precise measurement of the various serum components. The results revealed that co-infected patients exhibited significantly elevated levels of several key markers compared to reference values and the HIV-positive control group. Alkaline phosphatase levels were markedly high at 629.16 ± 9.88 IU/L, while gamma-glutamyl transferase levels were 70.66 ± 5.66 IU/L. Direct bilirubin was elevated at 14.15 ± 3.94 μmol/L, and total bilirubin was 20.05 ± 2.83 μmol/L. These findings suggest a cholestatic profile, which is characterized by the elevation of ALP, GGT, and bilirubin, indicating potential disruption in bile flow or liver function. Renal function markers also showed significant abnormalities in the co-infected group. Creatinine levels were notably high, with women averaging 199.65 ± 5.51 μmol/L and men averaging 245.68 ± 4.54 μmol/L. Urea levels were also elevated at 10.04 ± 1.15 mmol/L, although the p-value for this specific comparison was 0.25. The elevation of creatinine and urea suggests that the co-infection may have a synergistic effect on renal function, potentially leading to reduced kidney performance in these patients. In contrast to the elevated liver and renal markers, the study observed a significant decrease in total cholesterol levels among co-infected patients, with a mean of 2.81 ± 0.17 mmol/L. This hypocholesterolemia was significantly greater in co-infected patients than in those with HIV monoinfection, whose cholesterol levels were relatively normal. The researchers suggested that this decrease could be linked to the rapid progression of liver failure, such as cirrhosis, which is often associated with lower cholesterol levels in severely infected individuals. Discover more Study Biology Buy Maps Shop African Art Explore History Healthcare Interestingly, the levels of transaminases, specifically alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and aspartate aminotransferase (AST), were found to be within normal ranges for the co-infected patients. ALT levels were 20.05 ± 3.96 IU/L, and AST levels were 31.70 ± 8.86 IU/L. This finding contrasts with the typical expectation of elevated transaminases in liver disease. The authors explained that HIV-induced immunosuppression may limit the release of these liver enzymes into the blood, as the destruction of CD4+ T lymphocytes reduces the immune response that typically attacks HBV-infected hepatocytes. The study also noted that blood sugar levels were relatively normal in both co-infected and mono-infected patients, with a mean of 5.93 ± 0.66 mmol/L. Uric acid levels were increased in men compared to women, but co-infected men had GGT and urea levels that were closer to the healthy range compared to other groups. These observations highlight the complex interplay between the two viruses and their impact on various biochemical pathways in the body. The researchers acknowledged several limitations in the study, including the insufficient sample size, which was constrained by the high cost of laboratory reagents. They noted that a larger sample, ideally between 1,500 and 2,000 patients, would have provided more robust data. Additionally, the study did not measure IgM and anti-HBc antibodies to distinguish between acute and chronic infections, nor did it assess viral load or its impact on biochemical variations. Factors such as hepatitis C, delta virus, alcohol consumption, and hepatotoxic medications were not evaluated, which could have influenced the enzyme levels. The findings underscore the importance of monitoring biochemical parameters in patients with HIV-HBV coinfection. The distinct profile of hyperbilirubinemia, hypercreatinemia, and hypocholesterolemia suggests that these patients may be more vulnerable to hepatic and renal complications. The authors concluded that antiretroviral therapy should be accompanied by careful monitoring of these biochemical markers to manage the potential deterioration in overall health. This study provides valuable insights into the physiological effects of co-infection and highlights the need for tailored clinical approaches in managing these patients. Published in Discover Medicine, this research contributes to the growing body of evidence on the management of HIV-HBV coinfection. By identifying specific biochemical abnormalities, the study offers a framework for clinicians to better monitor and treat patients with this dual infection. The results emphasize the complexity of the disease and the necessity for comprehensive care that addresses both the viral and the metabolic aspects of the condition. To provide broader epidemiological context for their investigation, the researchers highlighted that human immunodeficiency virus and hepatitis B virus share major transmission pathways, including exposure to infected blood, sexual contact, and mother-to-child transmission. According to figures from the World Health Organization cited in the report, approximately 2.7 million individuals living with hepatitis B are co-infected with HIV, while an estimated 7.4 percent of people living with HIV globally also carry HBV. The authors noted that in the human body, urea serves as a marker of residual nitrogen generated through protein metabolism, reflecting renal filtration performance, while creatinine measures muscle breakdown products in circulation. Similarly, bilirubin functions as a bile pigment derived from hemoglobin catabolism that contributes antioxidant cellular protection, and alkaline phosphatase plays essential biological roles in both hepatic activity and bone formation. The study protocol incorporated specific diagnostic workflows and analytical assays to confirm infections and evaluate serum chemistry. Screening for HIV-1 and HIV-2 antibodies utilized the Abbott Determine immunochromatographic rapid test, recognized for 100 percent sensitivity and 99.72 percent specificity, followed by confirmation with the automated MINI VIDAS system. Surface antigen detection for hepatitis B was performed using the ONSITE HBsAg Combo immunoassay, exhibiting up to 100 percent sensitivity and specificity, and similarly confirmed by automated VIDAS testing. Biochemical determinations on the SELECTRA PROS analyzer relied on standardized enzymatic chemistries: glucose, uric acid, creatinine, and cholesterol were evaluated via enzymatic PAP end-point techniques, urea was measured through urease and glutamate dehydrogenase kinetics, and transaminases were assessed using kinetic formulations without pyridoxal phosphate in line with established international clinical chemistry recommendations. In interpreting the distinct enzymatic patterns observed in co-infected patients, the investigators contextualized their findings using established diagnostic models of liver injury. Clinical frameworks categorize hepatic enzyme elevations into hepatocellular patterns marked primarily by increased transaminases, cholestatic patterns defined by raised alkaline phosphatase and hyperbilirubinemia, and mixed injury patterns displaying elevations across all three markers. The data showed that co-infected patients predominantly exhibited a cholestatic profile alongside elevated gamma-glutamyl transferase, whereas HIV mono-infected patients more frequently presented with mixed enzyme disruptions. The authors noted that the immunotolerant phase of hepatitis B infection, coupled with HIV-mediated depletion of CD4 positive T lymphocytes, diminishes cytotoxic immune destruction of HBV-infected hepatocytes, which keeps circulating transaminase concentrations deceptively low despite ongoing hepatobiliary disturbance. The demographic findings were also compared with regional surveillance data from across the African continent. The co-infected cohort had an overall mean age of 36.83 years, spanning an age range from 17 to 62 years, which closely aligns with cohorts evaluated in Nigeria and Cameroon. Addressing the two-to-one female predominance in their sample, the authors highlighted recognized biological, structural, and socio-economic vulnerabilities, including larger mucosal surface area and heightened anatomical susceptibility, that contribute to multiple viral acquisitions among women. Furthermore, while earlier Gabonese research in Franceville had documented higher coinfection frequencies in men, the Libreville results mirrored gender patterns reported in Guinea and broader population trends where women represent a larger share of individuals living with HIV. View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted from August 1 to November 30, 2025, with the news report published on November 10, 2026.
Location and population
The study took place in Libreville, Gabon, involving patients co-infected with HIV and HBV, including men and women aged 16 years and older.
Event
A prospective, cross-sectional study identified a distinct biochemical profile in patients co-infected with HIV and HBV, published in Discover Medicine by Bioengineer.org.
Scale, impact and risk
Out of 560 HIV-positive patients, 60 were co-infected with HBV (10.71%). The co-infected group showed elevated levels of alkaline phosphatase, gamma-glutamyl transferase, direct and total bilirubin, creatinine, and urea, while total cholesterol was significantly decreased. These findings suggest a cholestatic profile and potential renal dysfunction, with implications for clinical management.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted at the Sino-Gabonese Cooperation Hospital, with biochemical parameters analyzed using spectrophotometric methods. The researchers recommended monitoring these markers in co-infected patients and adjusting antiretroviral therapy accordingly. The findings are based on a specific cohort and are not a public health outbreak.

FDA approves first antisense oligonucleotide therapy for chronic hepatitis B - The Herald Insight

News Brief
On October 20, 2026, the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen), an antisense oligonucleotide therapy for chronic hepatitis B. This approval represents a major advancement in the treatment of the condition, offering a new therapeutic option. The decision was announced in the context of broader discussions on AI healthcare and other topics at the World Bio Summit in Seoul. The evidence confirms the FDA's approval but does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. The event is reported as confirmed, with no indication of ongoing investigation or uncertainty. The approval is a significant milestone in managing chronic hepatitis B, potentially improving patient outcomes and treatment options.

Vir 的每月 Delta 型肝炎组合使 79% 的患者在 3 期试验中检测不到病毒 - 医学日报

Vir's Monthly Hepatitis Delta Combination Made the Virus Undetectable in 79% of Patients in Phase 3 Trial - Medical Daily
新闻简报Vir Biotechnology 公布了每月注射两种药物治疗丁型肝炎病毒的 3 期试验结果,显示 79% 的患者在 48 周后达到了检测不到的病毒水平。该试验名为 ECLIPSE 1,招募了 124 名患有慢性丁型肝炎的成年人,其中包括代偿性肝硬化患者。患者被随机分配立即开始 治疗 或等待 12 周。第 48 周时,79% 的治疗患者病毒水平检测不到,而延迟治疗组的病毒水平为 0%。此外,44% 的治疗患者肝酶水平达到正常,而对照组则没有这样的结果。Vir 报告的研究结果尚未在同行评审期刊上发表,突显了 治疗 对于这个经常被忽视的小群体的潜力。该试验还包括标准乙型肝炎 抗病毒药物 药丸作为治疗方案的一部分。这些结果意义重大,因为丁型肝炎被认为是最严重的病毒性肝炎,并且仅感染已经患有乙型肝炎的人。

美国,慢性丁型肝炎患者。

Vir Biotechnology 公布的 3 期试验结果显示,每月注射两种药物可使 48 周后 79% 的患者检测不到丁型肝炎病毒,而对照组则没有这样的结果。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vir's Monthly Hepatitis Delta Combination Made the Virus Undetectable in 79% of Patients in Phase 3 Trial - Medical Daily

News Brief
Vir Biotechnology announced results from a phase 3 trial on its monthly two-drug injection for hepatitis delta virus, showing that 79% of patients achieved undetectable virus levels after 48 weeks. The trial, named ECLIPSE 1, enrolled 124 adults with chronic hepatitis delta, including those with compensated cirrhosis. Patients were randomly assigned to start treatment immediately or wait 12 weeks. At week 48, 79% of treated patients had undetectable virus levels, compared to 0% in the delayed group. Additionally, 44% of treated patients reached normal liver enzyme levels, while the comparison group had no such results. The findings, reported by Vir and not yet published in a peer-reviewed journal, highlight the potential of the treatment for this small, often overlooked group. The trial also included standard hepatitis B antiviral pills as part of the regimen. These results are significant as hepatitis delta is considered the most severe form of viral hepatitis and infects only people who already have hepatitis B.

Innovation Vir's Monthly Hepatitis Delta Combination Made the Virus Undetectable in 79% of Patients in Phase 3 Trial Published Nov 11, 2026 2:35 AM EST By Joseph James howan Sulaiman Ali / Middle East Images | AFP via Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on WhatsApp A monthly two-drug injection made hepatitis delta virus undetectable in 79% of patients after 48 weeks in a phase 3 trial, Vir Biotechnology announced Nov. 9 at The Liver Meeting in Denver. None of the patients in a comparison group waiting to start treatment reached that result, according to the company's announcement. The trial's main goal was stricter. Undetectable virus combined with normal liver enzyme levels was reached by 44% of treated patients, compared with 0% in the delayed group. The results come from the company and have not yet been published in a peer-reviewed journal. The stakes are high for a small, often overlooked group. Hepatitis delta infects only people who already have hepatitis B, and it is considered the most severe form of viral hepatitis. The FDA approved the first U.S. treatment for it, Gilead's bulevirtide, in May. Inside the ECLIPSE 1 Results ECLIPSE 1 enrolled 124 adults with chronic hepatitis delta, including patients with compensated cirrhosis. They were randomly assigned two to one to start the combination of elebsiran and tobevibart right away, or to wait 12 weeks before starting. The comparison is not a standard placebo test, and that matters for reading the numbers. Treated patients were measured at week 48, while the delayed group was measured at week 12, before it began therapy. That design shows the drugs work against untreated infection, but it does not compare equal lengths of time. The trial was also open-label, meaning patients and doctors knew who was being treated, according to the study's ClinicalTrials.gov listing. Viral load is a lab measurement, which limits the risk of bias for that result, but it remains a design limit. The virus became undetectable in 85% of 46 treated patients with cirrhosis and 73% of 37 without it, Clinical Trials Arena reported. Liver enzyme levels returned to normal in 53% of treated patients, compared with 5% in the delayed group, according to the company. A Two-Drug Attack on Shared Viral Machinery Elebsiran is a small interfering RNA, a type of drug that silences genetic instructions the hepatitis B virus uses to make its surface protein. Hepatitis delta depends on that protein to build new virus particles and infect liver cells. Tobevibart is a monoclonal antibody, a lab-made immune protein that targets the hepatitis B surface protein. Together, the drugs attack machinery that both viruses need. Participants also took standard hepatitis B antiviral pills. "Our ambition in hepatitis D has always been to fundamentally change what patients can expect from treatment," said Vir CEO Marianne De Backer, Clinical Trials Arena reported. Evidence Check and Safety The company said the regimen was well tolerated. One patient had a severe, grade 3 drop in a type of white blood cell that resolved in 15 days, and no patients stopped or interrupted treatment. The most common side effect was flu-like symptoms, mostly after the first dose and usually gone within one to two days. The results do not yet show whether undetectable virus prevents cirrhosis, liver cancer, or death over years, or whether the effect lasts after treatment stops. Participants are scheduled to receive the drugs for up to 240 weeks, so longer-term data on liver outcomes should follow, though years of injections would also bring cost and access questions. In an earlier phase 2 study, all 10 patients in a small group who stopped after 96 weeks of suppression stayed suppressed 12 weeks later, but that group was tiny, and follow-up was short. Vir funded the trial and reported all figures. Independent experts have not yet reviewed the full data. The FDA has granted the combination Breakthrough Therapy and Fast Track designations, which can speed development and review but do not guarantee approval. Testing, Access and What Comes Next The people most likely to benefit are adults with chronic hepatitis B who also carry hepatitis delta, many of whom do not know it. In the United States, only about 8.5% to 12% of people with hepatitis B have been tested for delta in reported studies, according to a clinical review. Risk is higher among people who inject drugs and people from regions where the virus is common. People with hepatitis B can ask their clinician whether they have been tested for hepatitis delta, which starts with a blood antibody test. Those diagnosed can ask about referral to a liver specialist, about bulevirtide, which received accelerated FDA approval in May as a daily injection, and about clinical trials. Hepatitis B vaccination also prevents hepatitis delta, because the delta virus cannot infect someone without hepatitis B. Adults who are not vaccinated can ask a pharmacist or clinician about the series. Yellowing of the skin or eyes, a swollen belly, confusion or vomiting blood need urgent care. Vir plans to seek FDA approval in mid-2027. Results from ECLIPSE 2, which tests switching patients who did not respond to bulevirtide, and ECLIPSE 3, a head-to-head comparison with bulevirtide, are expected in early 2027. Price has not been announced. MedicalDaily will report the full publication and the FDA filing. The virus became undetectable in 79% of treated patients at 48 weeks, compared with 0% in the delayed group. What was the main goal? Undetectable virus plus normal liver enzymes, reached by 44% of treated patients. Who can get hepatitis delta? Only people who also have hepatitis B. Is there an approved treatment in the U.S.? Yes. Bulevirtide received accelerated FDA approval in May 2026. Vir's combination is not approved. Were there serious side effects? One severe lab abnormality resolved in 15 days, and no one stopped treatment. When could it be available? Vir plans an FDA submission in mid-2027. Approval is not guaranteed. 研究确定了 抗病毒药物 治疗 HBV 和 HCV 混合感染失败的预测因素

Study Identifies Predictors of Antiviral Treatment Failure in HBV and HCV Coinfection
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 10 日发表的一项研究确定了巴基斯坦同时感染乙型肝炎 (HBV) 和丙型肝炎 (HCV) 病毒的患者 抗病毒药物 治疗 失败的预测因素。该研究在拉瓦尔品第圣家医院进行,评估了 207 名接受为期 12 周索磷布韦和达拉他韦治疗的成年患者。研究发现,89.9% 的患者在第 4 周达到持续病毒学应答,其中 10.1% 的患者出现 SVR4 失败。到第 12 周,84.5% 的患者有持续缓解,而 15.5% 的患者经历了 SVR12 失败。治疗 失败的预测因素包括较高的体重指数、较高的病毒载量和男性。研究还指出,先前基于干扰素的治疗与较低的失败率有关。该研究使用机器学习模型(XGBoost 和随机森林)来预测结果,但作者强调在将这些模型应用于日常护理之前需要进行外部验证。该研究结果基于单个 队列研究,尚未推广。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年11月10日发表,临床评估在巴基斯坦拉瓦尔品第的圣家医院进行。治疗 后的观察里程碑是在第 4 周和第 12 周。
地点与对象
该临床评估在巴基斯坦拉瓦尔品第的圣家医院进行,涉及 207 名被诊断患有乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎并发感染的成年患者。
事件
一项回顾性 队列研究 研究评估了巴基斯坦乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎病毒合并感染患者的 抗病毒药物 治疗 结果和预测模型。该研究确定了 治疗 失败的预测因素,包括体重指数、病毒载量和患者人口统计数据。
规模、影响与风险
研究报告称,89.9% 的患者在第 4 周达到持续病毒学应答,其中 10.1% 的患者出现 SVR4 失败。到第 12 周,84.5% 的患者持续了病毒学应答,而 15.5% 的患者经历了 SVR12 失败。该研究强调了基线指标在预测 治疗 结果中的重要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项为期 12 周治疗方案的回顾性 队列研究 分析。使用的预测算法(XGBoost 和随机森林)被认为是探索性的,需要外部验证。该研究结果基于单一临床 队列研究,尚未适用于常规临床使用。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Study Identifies Predictors of Antiviral Treatment Failure in HBV and HCV Coinfection

News Brief
A study published on November 10, 2026, identified predictors of antiviral treatment failure in patients coinfected with hepatitis B (HBV) and hepatitis C (HCV) viruses in Pakistan. Conducted at Holy Family Hospital in Rawalpindi, the research evaluated 207 adult patients undergoing a 12-week regimen of sofosbuvir and daclatasvir. The study found that 89.9% of patients achieved a sustained virological response at week 4, with 10.1% experiencing SVR4 failure. By week 12, 84.5% had sustained response, while 15.5% experienced SVR12 failure. Predictors of treatment failure included higher body mass index, elevated viral load, and male sex. The research also noted that prior interferon-based therapy was linked to lower odds of failure. The study used machine learning models (XGBoost and Random Forest) to predict outcomes, but the authors emphasized the need for external validation before these models can be applied in routine care. The findings are based on a single cohort and are not yet generalizable.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Study Identifies Predictors of Antiviral Treatment Failure in HBV and HCV Coinfection by November 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Study Identifies Predictors of Antiviral Treatment Failure in HBV and HCV Coinfection Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Direct-acting antiviral treatment achieved high rates of viral clearance among patients coinfected with hepatitis B and hepatitis C viruses, according to a retrospective cohort study evaluating treatment outcomes and predictive models in Pakistan. The investigation focused on identifying baseline clinical, virological, and liver-fibrosis indicators that anticipate both early and sustained treatment non-response. While dual infection has historically complicated therapeutic management in endemic regions, twelve weeks of combined sofosbuvir and daclatasvir yielded sustained clearance in the clear majority of treated individuals. The clinical evaluation took place at Holy Family Hospital in Rawalpindi, Pakistan, and enrolled a cohort of 207 adult patients diagnosed with concurrent hepatitis B and hepatitis C infections. Every participant underwent a standard 12-week therapeutic regimen consisting of the direct-acting antiviral agents sofosbuvir and daclatasvir. To examine the trajectory of viral persistence, the researchers recorded all potential host and viral predictors prior to the start of treatment, establishing a rigorous baseline to evaluate subsequent therapeutic outcomes. Therapeutic success and failure were measured across two distinct post-treatment observation milestones. The research team defined sustained virological response failure at week four, designated as SVR4 failure, as the presence of detectable hepatitis C virus ribonucleic acid four weeks following the completion of therapy. Similarly, sustained virological response failure at week twelve, termed SVR12 failure, was defined as detectable viral ribonucleic acid measured twelve weeks post-treatment, serving as the benchmark for sustained therapeutic non-response. Primary outcome measurements demonstrated robust overall efficacy across the cohort despite the presence of complex dual viral infections. Sustained virological response at four weeks was achieved by 186 out of 207 patients, representing an early response rate of 89.9 percent, whereas 21 patients, or 10.1 percent, experienced SVR4 failure. By the twelve-week post-treatment mark, sustained virological response was confirmed in 175 individuals, corresponding to an 84.5 percent success rate, while 32 participants, or 15.5 percent of the cohort, experienced SVR12 failure. To uncover the variables linked to therapeutic non-response, the investigators performed univariable screening alongside exploratory multivariable logistic regression analyses. For early non-response at four weeks, multivariable modeling demonstrated that SVR4 failure was independently associated with a higher body mass index, higher baseline hepatitis C viral RNA levels, and male sex. In contrast, patient history or status with an interferon-based regimen was associated with lower adjusted odds of experiencing treatment failure at this early post-treatment benchmark. Multivariable evaluation of twelve-week outcomes identified overlapping and distinct clinical determinants associated with long-term viral clearance. SVR12 failure was significantly associated with older patient age, elevated body mass index, male sex, and higher pre-treatment viral load levels. Consistent with the four-week findings, status involving an interferon-containing therapeutic regimen was again associated with lower adjusted odds of therapeutic failure at the twelve-week evaluation point. Beyond conventional regression, the study deployed internally validated machine learning models developed separately to predict four-week and twelve-week outcomes. Model performance was evaluated using five-fold cross-validation, the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve, precision-recall area under the curve metrics, and SHAP feature importance analysis. The XGBoost algorithm achieved the highest precision-recall area under the curve for predicting SVR4 failure, whereas the Random Forest algorithm attained the highest precision-recall area under the curve for predicting SVR12 failure. SHAP interpretation highlighted pre-treatment viral RNA levels, patient age, body mass index, and fibrosis indices as the primary contributors guiding model predictions. Although fibrosis-related variables contributed substantially to descriptive observations and machine learning interpretations, the study authors emphasized that these predictive algorithms remain exploratory and require rigorous external validation in independent clinical cohorts before direct implementation in routine hepatitis care. 加纳监狱研究发现乙肝病毒高传染性标志物

Ghana Prison Study Finds High HBV Infectivity Markers
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 10 日发表在《BMC Infectious Drugs》上的一项研究分析了加纳惩教设施中 323 名 HBsAg 阳性囚犯的存档血浆样本。研究发现,这些样本中有 28.2% 的 HBeAg 呈阳性,表明 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染性增加,而 5.0% 的抗 HBc IgM 检测呈阳性,表明最近感染或重新激活。该研究强调了监狱内存在 HBV 传播 的可能性,但指出,横截面设计和缺乏监禁前数据阻碍了 传播 特定发生在设施内的确认。该研究建议通过 HBV DNA 检测进行纵向研究,以更好地了解 传播 模式。这些发现强调需要改进 HBV 筛查、传播 计划和惩教环境中的临床管理,以降低 传播 风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究是利用全国监狱调查期间收集的数据和生物样本进行的二次分析。发布日期为2026年11月10日。
地点与对象
该研究的重点是加纳惩教设施中的被监禁者,特别检查了 队列研究 中 323 名先前被确定为 HBsAg 阳性的人。
事件
对加纳被监禁者的存档血浆样本进行的横断面二次分析确定了与 乙型肝炎病毒 感染性和近期感染相关的大量 患病率 血清学标记。该研究由 Kristina Jarvis 于 2026 年 11 月 10 日报道。
规模、影响与风险
分析显示,323 份 HBsAg 阳性样本中,有 91 份 (28.2%) 呈 HBeAg 阳性,表明病毒感染性增加。此外,16 个样本 (5.0%) 的抗 HBc IgM 检测呈阳性,表明近期感染或重新激活。The study highlights the potential for HBV 传播 within correctional facilities but notes limitations in confirming intra-prison 传播 due to the absence of pre-incarceration serological data.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是作为二次分析进行的,具有固有的局限性。The researchers noted that the cross-sectional design and lack of pre-incarceration serological data prevent confirmation of intra-prison 传播.他们建议结合 HBV DNA 检测进行纵向研究,以更好地了解 传播 动态。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ghana Prison Study Finds High HBV Infectivity Markers

News Brief
A study published in BMC Infectious Diseases on November 10, 2026, analyzed archived plasma samples from 323 HBsAg-positive inmates in Ghanaian correctional facilities. It found that 28.2% of these samples were positive for HBeAg, indicating increased hepatitis B virus (HBV) infectivity, while 5.0% tested positive for anti-HBc IgM, suggesting recent infection or reactivation. The research highlights the potential for HBV transmission within prisons but notes that the cross-sectional design and absence of pre-incarceration data prevent confirmation of transmission occurring specifically within the facility. The study recommends longitudinal research with HBV DNA testing to better understand transmission patterns. These findings underscore the need for improved HBV screening, vaccination programs, and clinical management in correctional settings to reduce transmission risks.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Ghana Prison Study Finds High HBV Infectivity Markers by November 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Ghana Prison Study Finds High HBV Infectivity Markers Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A cross-sectional secondary analysis of archived plasma samples from incarcerated individuals in Ghana has identified a substantial prevalence of serological markers associated with hepatitis B virus infectivity and recent infection. The study, published in BMC Infectious Diseases, examined 323 samples from inmates who tested positive for the hepatitis B surface antigen, commonly known as HBsAg. The research aimed to evaluate the presence of hepatitis B e antigen, or HBeAg, and immunoglobulin M antibodies to hepatitis B core antigen, referred to as anti-HBc IgM, within this specific population. Correctional facilities are often recognized as high-risk environments for the transmission of blood-borne infections due to factors such as overcrowding, high-risk behaviors, and limited access to healthcare services. The study was conducted as a secondary analysis, utilizing data and biological samples collected during a nationwide prison survey. The researchers focused on a cohort of 323 individuals who had previously been identified as HBsAg-positive. The laboratory methods employed in this analysis involved enzyme-linked immunosorbent assays, or ELISAs, to detect the presence of HBeAg and anti-HBc IgM in the archived plasma samples. The results of the analysis revealed that 91 out of the 323 HBsAg-positive samples, representing 28.2 percent, were positive for HBeAg. This finding indicates that more than a quarter of the HBsAg-positive inmates in the study had serological evidence of increased viral infectivity. Additionally, 16 samples, or 5.0 percent, tested positive for anti-HBc IgM. This smaller proportion suggests that a subset of the population may have experienced recent infection or a reactivation of chronic disease. The statistical analysis further showed that HBeAg positivity was significantly associated with the type of prison and the duration of incarceration, with p-values less than 0.001 and 0.05, respectively. Regarding the anti-HBc IgM marker, the study found that most inmates who tested positive for this antibody had been incarcerated for more than one year. However, the researchers noted that no significant associations were observed between anti-HBc IgM positivity and the demographic variables examined in the study. The study design, being cross-sectional, has inherent limitations that the authors carefully addressed in their conclusion. In this context, the absence of pre-incarceration serological data precludes the confirmation that transmission occurred specifically within the prison. Although these findings highlight the potential for HBV transmission within correctional facilities, the cross-sectional design and absence of pre-incarceration serological data preclude confirmation of intra-prison transmission. The study suggests that strengthened HBV screening, vaccination programs, and clinical management are warranted to mitigate the risk of transmission. Furthermore, the researchers recommend longitudinal studies that incorporate HBV DNA testing to better characterize the dynamics of HBV transmission in correctional settings. 系统评价确定丙型肝炎是巴基斯坦肝癌的主要驱动因素 - Bioengineer.org

Systematic Review Identifies Hepatitis C as Pakistan’s Primary Liver Cancer Driver - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报在巴基斯坦进行的一项系统评价已确定丙型肝炎病毒是肝细胞癌的主要驱动因素,超过了 乙型肝炎病毒 和饮酒。该研究分析了 35 年的临床和流行病学数据,强调了成年男性中肝癌 发病率 的上升趋势。该研究包括 1990 年至 2025 年间发表的 98 项临床研究,发现丙型肝炎是该国肝癌的主要原因。然而,该审查指出了方法学的局限性,包括研究设计和数据收集的异质性,这导致人们对研究结果的信心中等。该研究强调需要加强临床干预、扩大筛查计划和积极治疗患者 监测,以解决巴基斯坦日益增长的肝癌负担。尽管做出了这些努力,治疗 的报告仍然不一致,并且获得肝移植和免疫疗法等先进疗法的机会受到地域限制。

时间
一项系统回顾评估了三十五年的临床和流行病学数据,确定丙型肝炎病毒是巴基斯坦肝细胞癌的主要病因驱动因素,远远超过 乙型肝炎病毒 和饮酒。

一项系统评价发现,丙型肝炎病毒是巴基斯坦肝细胞癌的主要驱动因素,其影响超过了 乙型肝炎病毒 和饮酒。

Systematic Review Identifies Hepatitis C as Pakistan’s Primary Liver Cancer Driver - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A systematic review conducted in Pakistan has identified hepatitis C virus as the primary driver of hepatocellular carcinoma, surpassing hepatitis B virus and alcohol consumption. The study analyzed thirty-five years of clinical and epidemiological data, highlighting the rising incidence of liver cancer among adult males. The research, which included 98 clinical studies published between 1990 and 2025, found that hepatitis C is the leading cause of liver cancer in the country. However, the review noted methodological limitations, including heterogeneity in study design and data collection, which led to a moderate confidence in the findings. The study emphasized the need for enhanced clinical intervention, expanded screening programs, and active patient surveillance to address the growing burden of liver cancer in Pakistan. Despite these efforts, treatment reporting remains inconsistent, and access to advanced therapies like liver transplantation and immunotherapy is geographically constrained.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Systematic Review Identifies Hepatitis C as Pakistan’s Primary Liver Cancer Driver by November 11, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Systematic Review Identifies Hepatitis C as Pakistan's Primary Liver Cancer Driver Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A systematic review evaluating thirty-five years of clinical and epidemiological data has identified hepatitis C virus as the primary etiological driver of hepatocellular carcinoma in Pakistan, far outpacing hepatitis B virus and alcohol consumption. Hepatocellular carcinoma represents the predominant primary liver cancer worldwide, accounting for seventy-five to eighty-five percent of hepatic malignancies and ranking as the sixth leading cause of cancer mortality. In 2022, approximately 865,269 new cases and 757,948 deaths occurred globally. In Pakistan, disease incidence has climbed steadily, placing liver cancer among the most frequently diagnosed malignancies in adult males. The new qualitative synthesis integrates evidence from ninety-eight clinical studies published between 1990 and 2025 to delineate national disease trends and viral contributions across diverse Pakistani patient populations and healthcare settings. Pathologically, hepatocellular carcinoma develops after protracted hepatic damage and extensive cirrhosis caused by viral infections, alcohol, or non-alcoholic fatty liver disease. Within cirrhotic tissue, dysplastic macronodules progressively advance into early hepatocellular carcinoma through multi-step hepatocarcinogenesis before progressing to advanced stages. In rare instances, tumors develop within healthy liver tissue, possibly arising from malignant transformation of hepatocellular adenomas. Clinical tumor detection relies heavily on elevated alpha-fetoprotein serum biomarkers across regional medical centers. Because underlying cirrhosis remains asymptomatic in most patients, early diagnosis proves challenging, resulting in delayed clinical presentation and increased mortality. Over the course of malignant progression, the diseased liver progressively loses its capacity to maintain enzymatic balance, ultimately culminating in fatal systemic failure unless appropriate clinical intervention occurs promptly in specialized tertiary hospitals. Discover more Read Med Journals Study Biology Find Treatments To clarify national trends, investigators conducted their analysis following the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses guidelines under prospective PROSPERO registration number CRD42023442582. The research protocol applied the Population, Intervention, Comparison, and Outcome framework, designating the Pakistani population as the study group and evaluating exposures to hepatitis B virus, hepatitis C virus, viral co-infection, and alcohol consumption to assess their relative contributions. The primary measured outcome was the occurrence of hepatocellular carcinoma across patient cohorts. The review authors executed an extensive literature search across ScienceDirect, PubMed, Google Scholar, Springer, and BMC from January 2022 through March 2025, evaluating English-language studies published between 1990 and 2025 using tailored Boolean expressions, Medical Subject Headings, and manual reference screening of eligible articles to ensure comprehensive retrieval. Discover more Find Cancer Care Check Symptoms cancer Cancer Database searches initially identified 539 citations, which underwent rigorous multi-stage eligibility screening. After removing 122 duplicate publications, title and abstract evaluations excluded 263 irrelevant articles. Full-text assessments were performed on 158 candidate studies, of which 57 were eliminated for lacking sufficient data aligned with review objectives, leaving 98 eligible studies involving advanced-stage disease cohorts. A formal quantitative meta-analysis was initially considered to determine pooled prevalence figures; however, substantial methodological heterogeneity precluded statistical aggregation. The included investigations showed wide discrepancies in study design, sample sizes, patient selection, diagnostic criteria, and geographic coverage, while several reports provided qualitative findings without numerical numerators and denominators necessary for pooled estimation, prompting a descriptive qualitative systematic review approach instead of a formal statistical meta-analysis. Methodological quality across the 98 incorporated studies was evaluated using the Quality in Prognostic Studies tool across six standard domains: study participation, study attrition, prognostic factor measurement, outcome measurement, confounding account, and statistical analysis. Overall, 41 studies exhibited low risk of bias, 38 moderate risk, and 19 high risk. Prognostic factor and outcome assessments demonstrated the lowest risk of bias because most studies employed established diagnostic standards for hepatic malignancy and viral infections. Conversely, confounding measurement showed the highest proportion of moderate-to-high risk ratings due to inadequate adjustment for age, sex, diabetes mellitus, and cirrhosis severity. These statistical reporting limitations and retrospective designs yielded an overall moderate confidence in the evidence, requiring cautious interpretation across regional healthcare settings. Among evaluated causes, chronic hepatitis C virus infection proved to be the primary cause of liver cancer across Pakistan, examined across 32 included studies. Globally, hepatitis C affects roughly 71.1 million people, but in Pakistan the blood-borne RNA virus maintains hyper-endemic transmission that imposes an immense clinical and economic toll. Transmission occurs primarily through contaminated blood products, unsafe medical injections, and inadequate equipment sterilization during healthcare procedures. With approximately 1.5 million blood donations collected annually in Pakistan, rigorous donor screening remains central to transmission control, particularly given estimated hepatitis C prevalence of 3.31 percent plus or minus 1.96 percent among healthy blood donors. Of the 32 dedicated hepatitis C studies, 21 confirmed that chronic infection induced hepatocellular carcinoma, 8 observed the highest incidence from hepatitis C, and only 3 reported a low incidence. Geographical analyses across Pakistan revealed pronounced regional differences in viral hepatitis and cancer distribution. Southern Pakistan showed hepatitis C and hepatitis B population prevalence rates of 1.84 percent and 1.7 percent, whereas northern areas recorded 1.3 percent and 2.3 percent, respectively. Center-specific studies reflected these patterns: Islamabad’s Nuclear Medicine Oncology and Radiotherapy Institute found 44.7 percent of liver cancer patients positive for hepatitis C, 13.1 percent for hepatitis B, and 4.3 percent co-infected. In Lahore, Shaikh Zayed Postgraduate Medical Institute observed hepatitis C in 75 percent, hepatitis B in 10 percent, and co-infection in 10 percent. Multan recorded 80 percent hepatitis C and 11 percent hepatitis B, while Muzaffargarh reported 67.3 percent hepatitis C, 5.9 percent hepatitis B, and 7.9 percent co-infection. Additional regional data illustrated continued hepatitis C predominance in northwest and southern centers alongside distinct localized exceptions. In Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, provincial research showed that 77.78 percent of liver cancer patients carried hepatitis C, 17.78 percent had viral co-infection, and only 4.44 percent had isolated hepatitis B. In Sindh, combined data from Isra University Hospital and Liaquat University Hospital in Hyderabad demonstrated 72.5 percent hepatitis C, 21.0 percent hepatitis B, 10.5 percent concurrent alcoholic liver disease with hepatitis C, and 6.5 percent viral co-infections. In Baluchistan, 7.5 percent of evaluated tumor patients had prior exposure to hepatitis B, hepatitis C, or co-infection. Conversely, Jinnah Postgraduate Medical Centre in Karachi documented hepatitis B in 67 percent of cancer patients and hepatitis C in 33 percent, demonstrating substantial epidemiological heterogeneity across geographic regions. Twenty included studies specifically evaluated hepatitis B virus contributions to hepatocellular carcinoma across the country. Globally, hepatitis B affects approximately 284 million individuals, with associated annual mortality climbing from 580,500 deaths in 1990 to 799,000 deaths by 2017. Although national blood donor surveys show donor hepatitis B prevalence between 2.41 percent and 1.96 percent, reflecting a gradual reduction, specific districts in northern Pakistan exhibit substantial endemicity. Reported regional rates include 13.35 percent in Mardan, 13.5 percent in Bannu, 14.15 percent in Peshawar, 14.15 percent in Kohat, 14.63 percent in Dera Ismail Khan, and 16.35 percent in Malakand. Among the 20 evaluated hepatitis B studies, 13 confirmed chronic infection induction, 5 reported it as the primary cause, and 2 noted low incidence, reflecting an epidemiological transition from earlier decades when hepatitis B predominated. Viral co-infection was examined in 20 studies, demonstrating that dual hepatitis B and hepatitis C exposure causes severe disease burdens. Multiple clinical cohorts documented significant co-infection proportions: Butt and colleagues identified 10 percent co-infection alongside 5 percent non-viral cases, while Farooqi and Farooqi recorded 17.78 percent. Other cohorts observed co-infection frequencies ranging from 2.44 percent to 10.3 percent, occasionally alongside hepatitis D or non-B non-C etiologies. Long-term demographic forecasts by Zahoor and colleagues projected hepatitis B and hepatitis C infection prevalence rates of approximately 3.25 percent and 6.36 percent by 2030. These ongoing projections indicate that sustained viral circulation will continue to fuel chronic liver disease and primary liver cancer across Pakistan without enhanced clinical intervention, expanded screening programs, and active patient surveillance. In sharp contrast to Western cohorts where alcohol abuse represents a major cause of liver malignancy, alcohol-induced hepatocellular carcinoma remains exceptionally rare in Pakistan. Only six studies evaluated alcohol, with Khan and colleagues reporting that merely 2 percent of cancer patients had an alcohol history, despite general observations that regular alcohol intake increases cancer risk by approximately 6 percent. Low alcohol-associated frequencies were consistently observed across three studies from Lahore and two from Sindh, while a Peshawar cohort identified alcohol-related disease in 3.2 percent of cases. Concurrently, emerging non-viral risks including metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease, obesity, type 2 diabetes, metabolic syndrome, and dietary aflatoxin B1 exposure causing TP53 gene mutations represent recognized global carcinogens, but empirical clinical research systematically evaluating their contribution in Pakistan remains remarkably scarce. Regarding clinical management, a subset of included studies reported interventions across tertiary medical centers, encompassing surgical resection, liver transplantation, local ablation, transarterial procedures, and systemic pharmacological therapies. Surgical intervention constituted the most frequently utilized therapeutic modality, documented in 1,820 managed cases, alongside microwave ablation, radiofrequency ablation, transarterial chemoembolization, transarterial radioembolization, and systemic drugs such as sorafenib, lenvatinib, and immune checkpoint inhibitors. However, treatment reporting varied substantially across published literature, frequently omitting tumor staging, allocation guidelines, and survival outcomes, while access to liver transplantation and advanced immunotherapy remains geographically constrained. Review authors concluded that addressing referral bias, establishing comprehensive national cancer registries, expanding hepatitis B vaccination, and widening direct-acting antiviral coverage will be critical to mitigating the expanding burden of hepatocellular carcinoma in Pakistan. 2025 年印度肝炎峰会探讨消除病毒性肝炎的国家途径 - Bioengineer.org

Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025 Examines National Pathways Toward Viral Hepatitis Elimination - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报The Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025, held in Chennai on January 17–18, 2025, brought together 54 program faculty and 275 delegates to examine national pathways toward eliminating viral hepatitis by 2030. The summit focused on hepatitis B and C, which account for 11.6 percent of the global burden, with about 29.8 million people living with hepatitis B and 5.5 million with hepatitis C in India. It reviewed global models, regional data, and strategies, including the National Viral Hepatitis Control Program launched in 2018 under the National Health Mission. The program has established 978 treatment sites across 711 districts and trained 4,900 master trainers. Delegates emphasized the need for collaboration with networks like the National AIDS Control Program and technical assistance from TREAT Asia to support same-day testing and treatment models. The summit also highlighted persistent social stigma and structural inequities, particularly affecting vulnerable populations such as women and individuals with disabilities, and underscored the importance of community-level engagements and digital tools in improving health literacy and care access.

India accounts for 11.6 percent of the global hepatitis B and C burden, with 29,800,000 people living with hepatitis B and 5,500,000 with hepatitis C. The summit highlighted that chronic hepatitis B and C infections are responsible for two-thirds of cirrhosis cases worldwide, with more than half of all cirrhosis cases in Southeast Asia stemming from these viruses. The disease burden places sustained pressure on regional healthcare delivery systems.

Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025 Examines National Pathways Toward Viral Hepatitis Elimination - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
The Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025, held in Chennai on January 17–18, 2025, brought together 54 program faculty and 275 delegates to examine national pathways toward eliminating viral hepatitis by 2030. The summit focused on hepatitis B and C, which account for 11.6 percent of the global burden, with about 29.8 million people living with hepatitis B and 5.5 million with hepatitis C in India. It reviewed global models, regional data, and strategies, including the National Viral Hepatitis Control Program launched in 2018 under the National Health Mission. The program has established 978 treatment sites across 711 districts and trained 4,900 master trainers. Delegates emphasized the need for collaboration with networks like the National AIDS Control Program and technical assistance from TREAT Asia to support same-day testing and treatment models. The summit also highlighted persistent social stigma and structural inequities, particularly affecting vulnerable populations such as women and individuals with disabilities, and underscored the importance of community-level engagements and digital tools in improving health literacy and care access.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025 Examines National Pathways Toward Viral Hepatitis Elimination by November 11, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025 Examines National Pathways Toward Viral Hepatitis Elimination Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram The Chennai Liver Foundation convened the first Indian Hepatitis Summit 2025 in Chennai on January 17–18, 2025, bringing together 54 program faculty and 275 delegates from government agencies, national and international organizations, and public and private hospitals. Published as a meeting report in BMC Proceedings, the summit examined the epidemiological landscape, programmatic frameworks, systemic barriers, and strategic pathways required to eliminate viral hepatitis as a public health threat in India by 2030, aligned with the World Health Organization Global Health Sector Strategies 2022–2030 and Sustainable Development Goal 3.3. Across 14 sessions featuring 46 talks and four panel discussions, participants evaluated global models, regional data from Southeast Asia, diagnostic advances, and community-level initiatives. The proceedings underscored that while India established the National Viral Hepatitis Control Program in 2018 under the National Health Mission, urgent operational hurdles persist with only five years remaining to meet global elimination targets. Epidemiological evidence presented at the summit indicates that India accounts for 11.6 percent of the global hepatitis B virus and hepatitis C virus burden, representing about 29,800,000 people living with hepatitis B and 5,500,000 people living with hepatitis C. Globally, viral hepatitis causes between 1.3 million and 1.4 million deaths annually, an impact strikingly identical to the mortality caused by tuberculosis. However, summit discussions highlighted two distinct characteristics of viral hepatitis mortality: fatalities are actively increasing, yet they remain preventable through existing interventions, specifically preventive hepatitis B vaccines and curative direct-acting antiviral therapies for hepatitis C. Across the broader World Health Organization Southeast Asia Region, an estimated 61.4 million individuals live with hepatitis B and 9.1 million live with hepatitis C. These figures highlight a substantial concentration of chronic viral infections that place sustained pressure on regional healthcare delivery systems. Within the Southeast Asia Region, the disease burden comprises 24 percent of global viral hepatitis cases, 10 percent of human immunodeficiency virus infections, and 16 percent of sexually transmitted infections. Chronic hepatitis B and hepatitis C infections are responsible for two-thirds of cirrhosis cases worldwide, and in Southeast Asia, more than half of all cirrhosis cases stem from these viruses, with a documented hepatitis B to hepatitis C ratio of 0.9. To inform Indian strategy, summit participants reviewed international operational models, highlighting Egypt’s successful hepatitis C program, which achieved widespread elimination through universal adult testing, extensive public awareness campaigns, digitized registries, and comprehensive patient follow-up. In addition, Pakistan’s Local Hepatitis Elimination and Prevention program illustrated the effectiveness of door-to-door screening for hepatitis B and hepatitis C paired with direct referral to clinical care, demonstrating practical decentralized approaches that could be adapted to Indian healthcare delivery. Discover more Check Symptoms Find Treatments Healthcare Health health India’s domestic response is anchored in the National Viral Hepatitis Control Program, launched in 2018 under the National Health Mission and modeled on earlier regional successes in the state of Punjab. That initiative, the Mukh Mantri Punjab Hepatitis C Relief Fund, established standardized diagnostic algorithms and treatment guidelines to address high prevalence rates, especially among intravenous drug users. The Punjab framework trained approximately 120 primary care physicians across 25 regional centers and deployed a tracking mechanism that alerted medical staff after two to three months of lost contact, thereby significantly reducing loss to follow-up. Scaling this design nationwide, the national program now maintains 978 treatment sites across 711 districts and has trained approximately 4,900 master trainers, granting treatment access to about 300,000 patients. Summit delegates emphasized that further expansion requires collaboration with established networks such as the National AIDS Control Program and technical assistance from TREAT Asia to support same-day testing and treatment models. Addressing viral hepatitis within vulnerable populations represented another central priority during the summit, where delegates examined persistent social stigma and structural inequities. Viral hepatitis frequently co-occurs with psychiatric conditions, including depression, schizophrenia, and borderline personality disorder, where stigma and reduced awareness substantially delay clinical diagnosis and medical care. Women’s health faces comparable neglect, with documented discrimination within healthcare settings, an absence of women-centric clinical pathways, distant testing facilities, and physiological diagnostic challenges such as transient reductions in alanine aminotransferase enzyme levels during pregnancy. Additionally, individuals with comorbidities and disabilities experience acute barriers to accessing care, as demonstrated by the VaccinEquity evaluation during the COVID-19 pandemic, which uncovered widespread obstacles including software applications that lacked visual accessibility, an absence of physical ramps or accessible transport, rigid binary queues, and discriminatory conduct by healthcare facility staff. These structural shortcomings highlight an urgent need for disability-inclusive health interventions and decentralized female-focused clinics nationwide. Discover more Get Health Tips Soothe Digestion Study Biology Tailored community strategies were proposed to support people who inject drugs, sex workers, and members of LGBTQIA+ communities, who frequently encounter profound stigma. For transgender individuals and sex workers, recommendations focused on establishing testing camps in institutional settings, scheduling flexible operational hours that accommodate work routines, training healthcare personnel, offering peer counseling, and maintaining dedicated support helplines. Among people who inject drugs, systematic data regarding hepatitis testing and treatment remain scarce because these individuals rarely access conventional hospital care. To mitigate transmission risks, the summit advocated harm reduction measures including needle-syringe exchange programs, oral opioid substitution therapy, reflex laboratory testing, and designated care coordinators. Crucially, clinicians stressed educating patients about post-cure reinfection risks, condom usage for prevention, periodic re-testing protocols to prevent diagnostic delays, and rapid antiviral intervention for acute hepatitis C reinfections. Nationally, overcoming these challenges will require stronger political commitment, robust data management, and sustained funding from central and state steering committees. Innovative solutions discussed at the meeting incorporated public health design thinking centered on four interactive activities: listening, observing, reading, and conducting ongoing dialogue with patients across care pathways. Community-level engagements, including educational street plays in rural regions, demonstrated practical value in raising health literacy. Similarly, community-based entities such as the Community Network for Empowerment have pioneered decentralized, same-day test-and-treat models alongside hepatitis vaccination campaigns tailored for people who inject drugs. In terms of clinical technology, transient elastography was identified as a critical point-of-care tool to detect asymptomatic liver fibrosis and cirrhosis that routine screenings overlook. However, pharmacological advances encounter substantial hurdles; the direct-acting antiviral combination glecaprevir/pibrentasvir demonstrates a 98 percent cure rate in non-cirrhotic hepatitis C genotype 3 over eight weeks and 84 percent over four weeks, but its utilization in India remains severely restricted due to patent barriers, exclusion from voluntary manufacturing licenses, and common patient reliance on traditional alternatives. Institutional innovations highlighted how decentralized care models can reach isolated settings, exemplified by programs developed at the All India Institute of Medical Sciences Rishikesh. Having established preventive hepatology clinics in 2021 and regular screening camps for fatty liver conditions, AIIMS Rishikesh implemented the Liver Induction Training and Update Program to train clinical practitioners. In a targeted prison micro-elimination initiative in Uttarakhand, medical teams deployed unmanned aerial drones to deliver hepatitis C therapeutics directly to ten inmates at Haridwar District Jail, while using drones to transport blood samples to diagnostic laboratories and conducting inmate clinical evaluations via telemedicine. Digital innovations featured prominently, notably the launch of the Hepat App by the Chennai Liver Foundation in collaboration with the Indian Council of Medical Research and the National Institute of Epidemiology. The application functions offline, meets Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources standards, aligns with the Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission, and supports clinical surveillance across viral hepatitis, diabetes mellitus, and hypertension. Participants emphasized that digital tools must be reinforced by user-friendly information systems, noting that the National Viral Hepatitis Control Program’s paperless management platform provides structured clinical modules and decision support. Delegates called for a national registry of healthcare professionals, along with the adoption of artificial intelligence chatbots for triage, voice-to-text documentation, and remote monitoring. To address severe capacity shortages, the Chennai Liver Foundation, ICMR, and NIE introduced an accredited online curriculum via learn.hepat.app aiming to train 10,000 healthcare workers. In clinical sessions addressing hepatocellular carcinoma, experts warned that although documented incidence appears low, liver cancer mortality in India is disproportionately elevated, with 80 to 90 percent of patients diagnosed too late for curative therapy. Driven by chronic hepatitis B, hepatitis C, non-alcoholic fatty liver disease, and alcohol consumption, liver cancer cases are projected to rise over two to three decades, necessitating integrated hepatology and alcohol clinics, early screening through tailors and barbers, and comprehensive palliative care linked to the national helpline 1800-202-7777. The summit concluded with comprehensive stakeholder commitments to accelerate national progress toward the 2030 viral elimination targets. Key priorities include strengthening national program implementation, boosting timely birth-dose hepatitis B vaccination and maternal interventions to reduce under-five hepatitis B prevalence to 0.1 percent or below, and securing stable supply chains for affordable vaccines, diagnostics, and antiviral drugs. Professional medical associations committed to training non-specialist clinicians, enhancing linkage to care, and integrating hepatitis services with primary care, HIV, tuberculosis, and maternal-child health programs. Civil society groups pledged to lead community-level awareness, combat discrimination, generate demand, and deliver decentralized peer support. Finally, private industry and pharmaceutical manufacturers agreed to invest in accessible point-of-care diagnostic tools, support educational campaigns, and establish innovative financing structures to reduce catastrophic out-of-pocket costs for individuals requiring treatment, solidifying a coordinated national framework to eliminate viral hepatitis across India.
