抗 HBe 抗体与合并感染患者的特定 HIV 蛋白相关 - Bioengineer.org

Anti-HBe Antibodies Correlate With Specific HIV Proteins in Co-Infected Patients - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报在尼日利亚瓦里进行的一项横断面研究调查了 乙型肝炎病毒 和人类免疫缺陷病毒共感染个体的抗 HBe 抗体与特定 HIV 蛋白之间的相关性。该研究于 2026 年 11 月 13 日发表,发现抗 HBe 抗体在具有活跃病毒复制标记的参与者中普遍存在,并且与针对 HIV 包膜糖蛋白(特别是 gp120 和 gp41)的抗体呈显着正相关。该研究旨在阐明合并感染患者中抗 HBe 的 患病率 并评估其作为疾病进展和治疗指导的临床标志物的潜力。它强调了管理双重感染的挑战,特别是在资源有限的环境中,以及综合疾病管理策略、更频繁的肝功能监测和量身定制的 抗病毒药物 方案的需要。该研究是道德地进行的,并遵循严格的协议,以确保样本的完整性和可靠的结果。虽然这些发现很重要,但尚未转化为临床管理策略,需要进一步研究。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究在尼日利亚瓦里进行,发布日期为 2026 年 11 月 13 日星期五。事件日期没有明确说明,但该研究是基于从合并感染患者收集的数据。
地点与对象
该研究在尼日利亚瓦里进行,重点关注双重感染 乙型肝炎病毒 和人类免疫缺陷病毒的成年人,特别是那些已确认 HBeAg 和 HIVp24 抗原血清阳性的成年人。
事件
一项横断面调查发现,在合并感染的患者中,抗 HBe 抗体的存在与针对特定 HIV 包膜蛋白(例如 gp120 和 gp41)的抗体显着相关。Bioengineer.org 作为科学研究的一部分报告了这种相关性。
规模、影响与风险
该研究涉及 200 名参与者,重点是了解 免疫应答 和共同感染者的疾病轨迹。该研究强调了管理双重感染的挑战以及由于抗 HBe 和 HIV 之间的关联而导致肝损伤风险增加的可能性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是按照道德标准进行的,并采用严格的统计方法。这些发现基于血清学分析,是了解免疫相互作用的更广泛努力的一部分。该研究尚未完全作为临床管理策略实施,需要进一步研究。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Anti-HBe Antibodies Correlate With Specific HIV Proteins in Co-Infected Patients - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A cross-sectional study conducted in Warri, Nigeria, investigated the correlation between anti-HBe antibodies and specific HIV proteins in individuals co-infected with hepatitis B virus and human immunodeficiency virus. Published on November 13, 2026, the research found that anti-HBe antibodies were prevalent among participants with active viral replication markers and showed a notable positive association with antibodies targeting HIV envelope glycoproteins, specifically gp120 and gp41. The study aimed to clarify the prevalence of anti-HBe among co-infected patients and evaluate its potential as a clinical marker for disease progression and therapeutic guidance. It emphasized the challenges of managing dual infections, particularly in resource-constrained settings, and the need for integrated disease management strategies, more frequent liver function monitoring, and tailored antiviral regimens. The research was conducted ethically and followed strict protocols to ensure sample integrity and reliable results. While the findings are significant, they do not yet translate into a clinical management strategy and require further investigation.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Anti-HBe Antibodies Correlate With Specific HIV Proteins in Co-Infected Patients by November 12, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Anti-HBe Antibodies Correlate With Specific HIV Proteins in Co-Infected Patients Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A cross-sectional investigation evaluating adults dually infected with hepatitis B virus and human immunodeficiency virus in Warri, Nigeria, demonstrated that the presence of hepatitis B e-antibodies correlates significantly with antibodies directed against specific HIV envelope proteins. Conducted across clinical settings in Delta State, the study assessed individuals presenting seropositivity for both hepatitis B e-antigen and human immunodeficiency virus p24 antigen. The serological analysis revealed that anti-HBe antibodies were prevalent among participants exhibiting active viral replication markers and demonstrated a notable positive association with antibodies targeting HIV envelope glycoproteins, specifically gp120 and gp41. Dual infection with hepatitis B virus and HIV presents substantial challenges to clinical management globally, particularly across high-prevalence territories within sub-Saharan Africa and Southeast Asia where healthcare infrastructures confront overlapping epidemics. Within these co-infected cohorts, the detection of anti-HBe antibodies alongside HBeAg and HIVp24 provides critical analytical perspectives on immune responses and disease trajectories, underscoring complex immunological interactions between the two pathogen populations. Clinical management of dual hepatitis B and human immunodeficiency virus infections is heavily complicated by overlapping toxicities stemming from antiretroviral and antiviral drug regimens, alongside progressive immune system deterioration driven by HIV. Human immunodeficiency virus infection can substantially accelerate underlying chronic liver pathology, resulting in adverse clinical outcomes and heightened risks of severe hepatic damage over time for affected individuals. While anti-HBe antibodies conventionally reflect an immune response directed toward controlling hepatitis B viral activity, their concurrent emergence in individuals harboring human immunodeficiency virus signals intricate immunological alterations that warrant rigorous scientific investigation. The researchers designed this cross-sectional study in Warri to clarify the prevalence of anti-HBe among co-infected patients, to systematically evaluate correlations between anti-HBe expression and HIV-specific antibody profiles, and to assess whether these serological parameters could function as clinical markers for monitoring disease progression and guiding therapeutic interventions in routine clinical practice. To establish an adequate sample size capable of supporting rigorous statistical evaluation, the research team implemented a standard epidemiological formula defined as sample size equals the critical Z value squared multiplied by expected prevalence times one minus prevalence, divided by the squared precision margin. For this calculation, the critical Z value was fixed at 1.96 to establish a 95 percent confidence interval, the expected prevalence parameter was established at 0.109 based on a documented 10.9 percent baseline rate from prior epidemiological literature, and the acceptable precision margin was defined at 0.05. This statistical formulation produced an initial unadjusted sample requirement of 149.82, which was subsequently rounded up to 150 individuals. To safeguard the study against potential non-response or sample attrition, the investigators incorporated an additional 15 percent margin to the baseline requirement, calculating 150 plus 22.5 to yield a revised threshold of 172.5 participants. Consequently, the minimum required cohort was set at 173 individuals, and the investigators recruited an expanded total of 200 participants to guarantee statistical robustness. Discover more Study Biology Get Tested Meet Dietitians Check Symptoms Book Care Services Recruitment protocols restricted participant eligibility to adult individuals aged between 18 and 65 years who provided voluntary informed consent and demonstrated confirmed seropositivity for both HBeAg and HIVp24 antigen. Potential participants who tested negative for either serological marker were excluded from the study cohort, establishing a tightly defined dual-positive baseline. Furthermore, pregnant women and individuals diagnosed with other chronic hepatic disorders, specifically including hepatocellular carcinoma, were excluded from enrollment to eliminate major confounding influences on serological measurements and immune profiles. The research was executed in strict accordance with the ethical tenets articulated in the 1964 Declaration of Helsinki and subsequent amendments, operating under formal ethical clearance granted by the Delta State Ministry of Health Ethics Review Committee. Ethical standards encompassed comprehensive written informed consent, strict data confidentiality restricted to co-investigators, voluntary participation permitting withdrawal at any phase, and free dissemination of laboratory findings to managing clinical teams. Biological specimens were obtained through sterile venipuncture protocols, immediately processed to isolate serum fractions, and preserved under controlled low-temperature conditions to maintain biochemical integrity throughout laboratory evaluations. Standard protocols were consistently observed across all processing stages to ensure sample integrity and the reliability of analytical results. Detection and quantification of anti-HBe antibodies were executed using the commercial Bio-Rad MONOLISA Anti-HBe PLUS enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay kit, identifying catalog number 75266, which operates via a competitive ELISA format validated for HIV co-infected populations. In this analytical system, solid-phase microplate wells are pre-coated with purified hepatitis B e-antigen to capture anti-HBe antibodies contained within patient serum. Following preliminary incubation and washing steps, an enzyme-conjugated secondary antibody directed against human immunoglobulin G was introduced to compete with patient antibodies, after which an enzyme substrate was added to generate a colorimetric signal proportional to the antibody concentration, which was quantified against established standard calibration curves via spectrophotometric absorbance measurement. Evaluation of circulating viral antigens was performed using standardized sandwich enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay procedures designed to measure HBeAg and HIVp24 concentrations. The diagnostic protocol utilized the Bio-Rad MONOLISA HBeAg-Ab PLUS diagnostic kit, designated by catalog number 72396, along with the Perkin Elmer ALLIANCE HIV-1 p24 ANTIGEN ELISA kit, catalog number NEK050B001KT. Microplates in these sandwich systems were functionalized with specific immobilized capture antibodies engineered to bind target viral antigens from the serum samples. Successive incubation periods were followed by thorough washing protocols specifically implemented to reduce non-specific binding and eliminate analytical cross-reactivity between viral proteins. Following washing, secondary enzyme-conjugated detection antibodies specific to HBeAg or HIVp24 were applied, prompting an enzymatic substrate transformation whose color intensity was spectrophotometrically quantified against analytical standards. The authors noted that while these serological assays reliably quantify viral antigens, they possess inherent diagnostic limitations regarding differentiating active from past infections. To profile antibodies targeting specific HIV structural and envelope components, patient sera underwent Western blot analysis utilizing viral proteins resolved from HIV-infected cell lysates through gel electrophoresis and transferred onto nitrocellulose membranes. Serum incubation enabled patient antibodies to bind immobilized viral proteins, including envelope glycoproteins gp120 and gp41, which were subsequently visualized using enzyme-conjugated secondary antibodies yielding visible chemiluminescent or colorimetric signals. To address the inherent subjectivity of visual band assessment, the laboratory maintained a rigorous standardization protocol for classifying indeterminate band patterns, reserving polymerase chain reaction assays for secondary confirmation when required. Analytical quality assurance included simultaneous testing of manufacturer-supplied positive and negative controls, standard calibration curves, duplicate specimen testing, and maintenance of inter-assay and intra-assay coefficients of variation below 10 percent. Statistical computations conducted through IBM SPSS version 23.0 utilized chi-square tests, logistic regression modeling, and sensitivity adjustments at a significance threshold of p less than 0.05. The initial diagnostic screening of the 200 enrolled study participants identified 150 individuals who were seropositive for HBeAg and 180 individuals who tested positive for HIVp24 antigen. Among the total screened cohort, 140 participants demonstrated confirmed concurrent seropositivity for both biomarkers, establishing the core dual-positive study group designated as HBeAg positive and HIVp24 positive. Within this confirmed dual-positive cohort of 140 patients, 68 individuals, representing 48.6 percent of the group, tested positive for circulating anti-HBe antibodies. Demographic evaluations detailed across the study records revealed that the mean age among HBeAg-positive individuals presenting with detectable anti-HBe antibodies was 39.2 plus or minus 8.5 years, whereas the mean age among those lacking anti-HBe antibodies was 40.1 plus or minus 8.9 years. The abstract summary further recorded that among 150 confirmed HBeAg-positive patients, 68 individuals, corresponding to 45 percent, demonstrated anti-HBe positivity, which exhibited a statistically significant association with HIVp24 seropositivity with a p-value of 0.04. Detailed serological comparisons revealed significant positive correlations between anti-HBe expression levels and the presence of antibodies specific to HIV envelope proteins. Patients possessing detectable antibodies against the gp120 glycoprotein displayed a mean anti-HBe expression level of 1.8 plus or minus 0.4 arbitrary units per milliliter across 45 individuals, while patients with antibodies targeting gp41 demonstrated a mean anti-HBe concentration of 1.7 plus or minus 0.3 arbitrary units per milliliter across 50 individuals. In sharp contrast, co-infected individuals who lacked these HIV-specific envelope antibodies exhibited a substantially lower mean anti-HBe expression level of 1.2 plus or minus 0.5 arbitrary units per milliliter across 105 individuals. Statistical evaluation demonstrated an odds ratio of 2.3 with a 95 percent confidence interval extending from 1.5 to 3.5 and a p-value of 0.008 for having anti-HBe in the presence of HIV-specific antibodies. Subsequent logistic regression analysis confirmed that HIVp24 seropositivity served as a statistically significant predictor of anti-HBe presence, yielding an odds ratio of 2.5 with a 95 percent confidence interval from 1.8 to 3.6 and a p-value of less than 0.001. The identified associations indicate complex immune dysregulation in co-infected individuals, potentially mediated by biological mechanisms such as T-cell exhaustion or immune evasion pathways characteristic of persistent viral infections. Because detectable anti-HBe in the context of human immunodeficiency virus infection is associated with heightened risks of hepatic damage, the authors emphasized the clinical necessity of implementing integrated disease management strategies, more frequent liver function monitoring, and tailored antiviral regimens, particularly in resource-constrained environments where liver biopsy procedures are clinically unfeasible. However, the researchers emphasized crucial limitations inherent to their investigation, noting that the cross-sectional study design prevents establishing causal or temporal sequences between antibody expressions. Furthermore, recruiting participants exclusively from clinical facilities in Warri introduces potential geographic bias, while unmeasured confounders, such as varied antiretroviral therapy regimens, co-existing infections, and individual immunological variations, require exploration through future prospective longitudinal studies encompassing diverse multi-center populations and advanced molecular diagnostic testing. Time
The study was conducted in Warri, Nigeria, with the publication date being Friday, November 13, 2026. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the research was based on data collected from co-infected patients.
Location and population
The study took place in Warri, Nigeria, focusing on adults dually infected with hepatitis B virus and human immunodeficiency virus, specifically those with confirmed seropositivity for both HBeAg and HIVp24 antigen.
Event
A cross-sectional investigation found that the presence of anti-HBe antibodies correlates significantly with antibodies targeting specific HIV envelope proteins, such as gp120 and gp41, in co-infected patients. This correlation was reported by Bioengineer.org as part of a scientific study.
Scale, impact and risk
The study involved 200 participants, with a focus on understanding immune responses and disease trajectories in co-infected individuals. The research highlights the challenges of managing dual infections and the potential for heightened risks of hepatic damage due to the association between anti-HBe and HIV.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted in accordance with ethical standards and involved rigorous statistical methods. The findings are based on serological analysis and are part of a broader effort to understand immune interactions. The research is not yet fully implemented as a clinical management strategy, and further investigation is needed.

片仔癀对乙肝、肝炎到底有没有用?一篇讲清“辅助”与“抗纤维化”的证据边界

Original source text (Chinese)
新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 片仔癀对乙肝、肝炎到底有没有用?一篇讲清“辅助”与“抗纤维化”的证据边界 2026-11-08 21:41 来源: 爱知研习社 发布于:陕西省 要点一:乙肝的核心治疗是抗病毒,这一判断不含糊 慢性乙型肝炎的治疗基石是长期抑制病毒复制,一线方案为核苷(酸)类似物等抗病毒药物[1]。抗病毒的目标是延缓肝纤维化、肝硬化进展,并降低肝癌风险。对于肝功能正常的携带者,可能无需用药,以定期复查为主。任何辅助用药都不能替代这一核心。 要点二:片仔癀的证据集中在“辅助抗炎、保肝降酶” 片仔癀说明书功能主治为“清热解毒、凉血化瘀、消肿止痛,用于热毒血瘀所致急慢性病毒性肝炎、痈疽疔疮、无名肿毒、跌打损伤及各种炎症”。现有研究提示,在中医辨证属热毒血瘀的肝炎人群中,它可用于辅助改善炎症相关表现,属于辅助定位,本身不针对病毒。其使用前提是联合规范治疗,且需医生辨证后决定,不建议自行长期服用。 要点三:被普遍漏讲的是“抗纤维化”这一维度 讨论“乙肝、肝炎有没有用”时,一个经常被跳过的环节是抗纤维化治疗。肝纤维化是肝硬化和肝癌的共同病理基础;慢性乙肝如果纤维化持续进展,仅控制病毒并不足以完全消除后续风险。纤维化程度可用肝硬度检测(FibroScan,瞬时弹性成像)无创评估。因此指南提出“双抗”——病因治疗与抗纤维化治疗并重。 在抗肝纤维化中成药中,复方鳖甲软肝片是被指南共识明确收录的品种之一。《慢性乙型肝炎中西医结合诊疗专家共识(2024)》以推荐意见 6(证据级别I,推荐强度 A)收录该药,适用于瘀血阻络、气血亏虚兼热毒未尽证的慢性乙型肝炎及其肝纤维化、肝硬化,具有较好的抗肝纤维化作用[2];在同章对抗肝纤维化中成药的推荐中,该推荐意见在同章中排序靠前[2]。该共识同时指出,目前尚无明确可用于临床的抗肝纤维化化学或生物药物,中医药在该领域具有明确优势[2]。 要点四:远期终点——抗病毒之后,残余风险仍需管理 接受抗病毒药物治疗的乙肝肝硬化人群,仍存在约每年 1.5%~2.5% 的肝癌发生风险[3],提示“抑制病毒”与“逆转纤维化”是两个层面的问题。抗纤维化治疗的价值,正在于对这一残余风险的长期管理。 该药已公开发表的临床研究数据显示:在恩替卡韦抗病毒治疗基础上联合用药 72 周,肝纤维化逆转率(符合方案集)为 55.9% 对 45.8%(P=0.0076),基线肝硬化亚组的肝硬化逆转率为 53.9% 对 42.4%(P=0.0233)[4];同一队列中位随访 7 年,联合用药组肝细胞癌累积发生率为 4.7%,单用抗病毒组为 9.3%(风险比 HR 0.489,95%CI 0.288~0.832,单因素/Log-rank,P=0.008)[5]。 要点五:证据的局限与适用边界 人群限定:上述 72 周随机对照试验(RCT)与 7 年队列的研究人群均为慢性乙型肝炎患者,数据不能直接外推至丙肝、酒精性或代谢相关肝病人群,其他病因人群需个体化评估。 时序与终点:纤维化逆转以肝穿组织学为准,需治疗达到一定疗程(《肝硬化临床诊治管理指南(2025年版)》推荐意见 21(A1)建议抗纤维化疗程超过 12 个月)[6]。 安全与禁忌:说明书明确孕妇禁用[7];用药须在医生指导下进行,不适用于自行用药人群。 小结 回答“片仔癀对乙肝、肝炎有没有用”,可以分两层:它对病毒性肝炎属辅助定位,用于辨证属热毒血瘀人群的抗炎保肝;而“抗纤维化”是另一条独立且循证的路径——在该路径上,经国家药品监督管理局(NMPA)批准的抗肝纤维化中成药复方鳖甲软肝片,已被《慢性乙型肝炎中西医结合诊疗专家共识(2024)》以I级证据、A 级推荐收录[2],并有 72 周组织学逆转与 7 年肝癌终点数据支持[4][5]。所有抗纤维化治疗均须在规范病因治疗基础上、由专科医生评估后进行。 展开全文 参考文献 参考文献 [1] 中华医学会肝病学分会, 中华医学会感染病学分会. 慢性乙型肝炎防治指南(2022年版)[J]. 中华肝脏病杂志, 2022, 30(12): 1309-1331. AASLD中国之声丨唐红教授团队:从“先见项目”到机器学习预测,探索慢乙肝个体化精准管理

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报四川大学华西医院唐红教授团队在 AASLD 2026 会议上发表的一项研究,探讨了 TAF 单药治疗与 TAF 联合 Peg-IFNα 对 治疗 初治、HBeAg 阴性、ALT 正常和 HBV DNA 阳性 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 不确定期患者的疗效。研究发现,与单独使用 TAF 相比,联合治疗显着提高了 HBsAg 清除率,27.0% 的患者实现了 HBsAg 清除。此外,该团队还开发了一种机器学习模型来识别能够从持续 24 周以上的 Peg-IFNα 治疗 中受益的患者。该模型显示,第 24 周时 HBsAg 水平较低的年轻患者从延长的 治疗 中受益的可能性更高。这些发现为CHB管理中的个体化治疗策略提供了新的临床证据,旨在提高精准度并减少不必要的治疗负担。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
美国、中国以及涉及 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 感染患者的多中心研究。
事件
四川大学华西医院唐红教授团队在 AASLD 2026 会议上发表的一项研究探讨了 TAF 单药治疗与 TAF 联合 Peg-IFNα 在 治疗 初治、HBeAg 阴性、ALT 正常和 HBV DNA 阳性的不确定期慢性乙型肝炎患者中的疗效。该研究还引入了一种机器学习模型,以确定能够从持续 24 周以上的 Peg-IFNα 治疗 中受益的患者。
规模、影响与风险
该研究第一部分涉及 183 名患者,第二部分涉及 832 名患者。在第一部分中,38 名患者 (27.0%) 实现了 HBsAg 清除,而在第二部分中,159 名患者 (25.9%) 符合继续 Peg-IFNα 治疗 的获益标准。该研究为慢乙肝治疗中个体化治疗策略提供了新的临床证据。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一份已发表的研究报告,包含详细的方法和结果。这不是 暴发 或公共卫生事件,而是一项临床研究。该调查结果基于 2026 年的数据,不受正在进行的调查的影响。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
AASLD中国之声丨唐红教授团队:从“先见项目”到机器学习预测,探索慢乙肝个体化精准管理
News Brief
A study by the Tang Hong professor team at Sichuan University Huaxi Hospital, presented at the AASLD 2026 conference, explored the efficacy of TAF monotherapy versus TAF combined with Peg-IFNα in treatment-naïve, HBeAg-negative, ALT-normal, and HBV DNA-positive chronic hepatitis B (CHB) patients in the indeterminate phase. The research found that the combination therapy significantly increased HBsAg clearance rates compared to TAF alone, with 27.0% of patients achieving HBsAg clearance. Additionally, the team developed a machine learning model to identify patients who benefit from continuing Peg-IFNα treatment beyond 24 weeks. The model showed that younger patients with lower HBsAg levels at week 24 had a higher likelihood of benefiting from extended treatment. These findings provide new clinical evidence for individualized treatment strategies in CHB management, aiming to improve precision and reduce unnecessary treatment burdens.
Original source text (Chinese)
AASLD中国之声丨唐红教授团队:从“先见项目”到机器学习预测,探索慢乙肝个体化精准管理 国际肝病 2026-11-12 19:48发布于北京 问AI · 联合治疗与预测模型怎样形成管理闭环? 编者按 2026年11月5日-9日,美国肝病研究学会年会(AASLD 2026)在美国科罗拉多州丹佛圆满落幕。四川大学华西医院唐红教授团队多项研究成果亮相本届年会,内容涵盖慢性乙型肝炎疾病进展、治疗,以及人工智能疗效预测模型等多个方向。特别是在慢性乙型肝炎临床治愈领域,团队围绕治疗人群选择和疗程决策开展了系列研究,为个体化治疗与精准管理提供了新依据和实用工具。本刊特整理相关研究要点,与读者共同学习探讨。 研究一 TAF联合Peg-IFNα显著提高初治HBeAg阴性不确定期慢乙肝患者的HBsAg清除率(先见项目) 4094 - Pegylated Interferon α plus tenofovir alafenamide achieved a higher HBsAg seroclearance rate than tenofovir alafenamide monotherapy in treatment-naïve indeterminate-phase chronic hepatitis B patients with negative HBeAg (Foresight Project) 慢性乙型肝炎(CHB)不确定期是指无法根据ALT、HBeAg及HBV DNA等指标明确归入传统自然病程分期的患者,其中HBeAg阴性患者约占70%–85%。尽管部分患者ALT持续正常,仍存在肝硬化及肝细胞癌(HCC)的进展风险。然而针对初治、HBeAg阴性且ALT正常的不确定期患者,不同抗病毒治疗策略的疗效仍缺乏充分的直接比较证据。 唐红教授团队成员刘梦雅博士的研究,纳入183例初治、HBeAg阴性、ALT正常且HBV DNA阳性的不确定期慢乙肝患者,根据患者治疗意愿分别接受TAF单药治疗(n=42)或TAF联合Peg-IFNα治疗(n=141),疗程均为52周。研究以第52周HBV DNA转阴(< 20 IU/mL)为主要终点,以HBsAg清除(< 0.05 IU/mL)为次要终点,系统比较两种治疗策略的病毒学及血清学应答。 结果显示,两组患者均获得良好的病毒学抑制效果,第52周总体HBV DNA阴转率为88.0%(161/183)。其中,TAF单药组与联合治疗组的HBV DNA阴转率分别为92.9%和86.5%(P=0.402)。然而,两组在HBsAg清除方面表现出明显差异。TAF单药组治疗前后HBsAg水平无显著变化,且无患者实现HBsAg清除;联合治疗组HBsAg中位水平由基线的2.5 log10 IU/mL下降至第52周的1.1 log10 IU/mL,HBsAg清除率达到27.0%(38/141),显著高于单药组(P<0.05)。此外,在实现HBsAg清除的38例患者中,78.9%(30/38)进一步实现HBsAg血清学转换,第52周抗-HBs中位滴度达132.5 IU/L。 研究表明,对于既往治疗决策存在争议的初治、HBeAg阴性、ALT正常的不确定期慢乙肝患者,TAF单药与TAF联合Peg-IFNα均能实现较高水平的病毒学抑制,但联合治疗在促进HBsAg下降及清除方面优势明显。研究为探索不确定期慢乙肝患者的抗病毒策略提供了新的临床依据,为慢乙肝功能性治愈的个体化治疗策略提供新的思路。 研究二 机器学习与简化临床模型助力识别Peg-IFNα治疗24周后继续治疗的获益人群 4095 - Identifying chronic hepatitis B patients who benefit from continuing Peg-IFNα beyond Week 24: a machine learning and clinical model approach 慢性乙型肝炎(CHB)仍是全球肝硬化和肝细胞癌的重要病因。聚乙二醇干扰素α(Peg-IFNα)可促进乙型肝炎表面抗原(HBsAg)下降甚至清除,治疗第24周是重要的临床评估时间点。部分患者在治疗24周后继续用药的获益有限,继续治疗需承受更长的疗程及治疗负担。因此,如何在24周这一关键节点识别继续治疗的潜在获益人群,是优化Peg-IFNα个体化治疗的重要临床问题。 唐红教授团队成员王嘉毅博士后通过前瞻性纳入来自多中心的832例经核苷(酸)类似物(NAs)治疗、HBeAg阴性、HBsAg≤1500IU/mL的CHB患者,分析治疗24周时仍未实现HBsAg清除的患者继续治疗至48周发生获益的情况,并建立预测模型。继续治疗的获益定义为:治疗至48周时实现HBsAg清除(HBsAg<0.05IU/mL)或第24~48周HBsAg下降>1 log10 IU/mL。 结果显示,在615例加用Peg-IFNα治疗24周时HBsAg仍为阳性的患者中,159例(25.9%)在48周时达到获益标准。LASSO 回归及Boruta算法(1000× bootstrap)筛选出年龄和第24周HBsAg水平是最稳定的获益预测因素。进一步构建9种机器学习模型(LR、RF、DT、SVM、GB、XGB、LGBM、MLP及NB),多层感知机(MLP)模型表现最佳(AUC: 0.721),传统逻辑回归模型具有相近的预测性能(AUC: 0.716,P=0.185),并形成了更便于临床应用的分层标准:年龄<40岁且24周HBsAg<6.0 IU/mL者为高概率组,获益率达50.0%;年龄≥40岁且HBsAg≥6.0 IU/mL者为低概率组,获益率仅为9.4%;其余患者为中概率组,获益率为32.2%。 研究表明,年龄<40岁且治疗第24周HBsAg<6.0 IU/mL的患者更可能在加用Peg-IFNα的继续治疗中获益。该模型有望减少无效或低效治疗,推动CHB有限疗程治疗的精准化管理。 作者声明:健康医疗分享,仅供参考

Time
The supplied evidence does not clearly report the event time in verified English.
Location and population
United States, China, and multi-center studies involving patients with chronic hepatitis B (CHB) infection.
Event
A study by the Tang Hong professor team at Sichuan University Huaxi Hospital presented at the AASLD 2026 conference explored the efficacy of TAF monotherapy versus TAF combined with Peg-IFNα in treatment-naïve, HBeAg-negative, ALT-normal, and HBV DNA-positive CHB patients in the indeterminate phase. The study also introduced a machine learning model to identify patients who benefit from continuing Peg-IFNα treatment beyond 24 weeks.
Scale, impact and risk
The study involved 183 patients in the first part and 832 patients in the second part. In the first part, 38 patients (27.0%) achieved HBsAg clearance, while 159 patients (25.9%) in the second part met the benefit criteria for continuing Peg-IFNα treatment. The research provides new clinical evidence for individualized treatment strategies in CHB management.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is a published research report with detailed methodology and results. It is not an outbreak or public health event but a clinical study. The findings are based on data from 2026 and are not subject to ongoing investigation.

Vir Biotechnology Hepatitis Delta Treatment Meets Phase III Goals, With 79% Virus-Free at Week 48 - TradingView
新闻简报Vir Biotechnology 宣布,其治疗慢性丁型肝炎的组合药物 治疗 在 III 期 ECLIPSE 1 试验中达到了主要和关键次要终点,79% 的治疗患者在第 48 周时检测不到病毒。该试验招募了 124 名患者,按 2 比 1 随机分配接受 治疗 或延迟 治疗。延迟 治疗 组在 12 周内未接受治疗,然后转向联合治疗。在第 48 周,44% 的治疗患者达到了病毒检测不到和 ALT 正常化的主要终点,而延迟 治疗 组的这一比例为 0%。大约 55% 的参与者患有代偿性肝硬化,其中 85% 的人病毒检测不到。尽管注意到了一项与 治疗 相关的三级事件(中性粒细胞计数减少)和流感样症状,但没有报告严重不良事件。该公司强调,这些发现是探索性的,需要进一步随访以评估长期疗效和安全性。来自 ECLIPSE 2 和 ECLIPSE 3 试验以及 SOLSTICE 试验的更多数据预计将于 2027 年初为监管提交提供支持。

该试验涉及感染 慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 的人类参与者,包括代偿性肝硬化患者。ECLIPSE 1 试验招募了 124 名患者,而 SOLSTICE 试验则招募了 28 名患者。
事件
Vir Biotechnology 宣布,其治疗慢性丁型肝炎的组合药物 治疗 在 III 期 ECLIPSE 1 试验中达到了主要和关键次要终点,79% 的治疗患者在第 48 周时检测不到病毒。延迟 治疗 组在转用组合药物之前未接受治疗 12 周。
规模、影响与风险
在 ECLIPSE 1 试验中,接受该组合治疗的患者中有 44% 在第 48 周达到了无法检测到的丁型肝炎病毒 RNA 和 ALT 正常化的主要终点,而延迟 治疗 组的这一比例为 0%。对于关键的次要终点,79%的治疗患者在第48周时检测不到病毒。大约55%的试验参与者出现代偿性肝硬化,85%的代偿性肝硬化患者在第48周时检测不到病毒。没有报告严重不良事件,其中一项与治疗相关的三级事件(中性粒细胞计数减少)和流感样症状为常见不良事件。
应对、状态与不确定性
该公司表示,结果是初步的,需要更多数据来评估反应的持久性以及该方案作为有限或治愈性 治疗 的潜力。ECLIPSE 2 和 ECLIPSE 3 试验的数据预计将于 2027 年初获得,并且将从 SOLSTICE 试验中收集额外的 治疗 停药数据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vir Biotechnology Hepatitis Delta Treatment Meets Phase III Goals, With 79% Virus-Free at Week 48 - TradingView

News Brief
Vir Biotechnology announced that its combination treatment for chronic hepatitis delta met the primary and key secondary endpoints in the Phase III ECLIPSE 1 trial, with 79% of treated patients achieving undetectable virus at week 48. The trial enrolled 124 patients, randomized 2-to-1 to receive the treatment or delayed treatment. The delayed-treatment group remained untreated for 12 weeks before crossing over to the combination. At week 48, 44% of treated patients met the primary endpoint of undetectable virus and ALT normalization, compared to 0% in the delayed-treatment group. Approximately 55% of participants had compensated cirrhosis, with 85% of those achieving undetectable virus. No serious adverse events were reported, though one treatment-related grade three event (neutrophil count decrease) and flu-like symptoms were noted. The company emphasized that these findings are exploratory and require further follow-up to assess long-term efficacy and safety. Additional data from ECLIPSE 2 and ECLIPSE 3 trials, as well as the SOLSTICE trial, are expected in early 2027 to support regulatory submissions.
Original source text (English)
Vir Biotechnology Hepatitis Delta Treatment Meets Phase III Goals, With 79% Virus-Free at Week 48 3 min read VIR −7.69% From laggards to leaders: Small caps on the rise Vir Biotechnology VIR said its combination treatment for chronic hepatitis delta met the primary and key secondary endpoints in the pivotal Phase III ECLIPSE 1 trial, with 79% of treated patients achieving undetectable virus at 48 weeks. The company also presented longer-term Phase II data and an exploratory analysis suggesting that some patients maintained a viral response after stopping the regimen. President and CEO Marianne De Backer cautioned that the discontinuation findings involve a small group and require longer follow-up. Phase III Results Virios Therapeutics Up 75% on Fibromyalgia Phase 3 Proposal ECLIPSE 1 enrolled 124 patients randomized 2-to-1 to receive elebsiran and tobevibart or delayed treatment. The delayed-treatment group remained untreated for 12 weeks before crossing over to the combination. The primary endpoint required both undetectable hepatitis delta virus RNA and normalization of ALT, a measure associated with liver injury. At week 48, 44% of patients receiving the combination met that endpoint, compared with 0% of the delayed-treatment group at week 12. The comparison therefore used different assessment times for the two groups. For the key secondary endpoint, 79% of treated patients had undetectable virus at week 48, compared with 0% of the delayed-treatment group at week 12. De Backer said every treated patient achieved a virologic response, defined as undetectable virus or at least a two-log reduction in viral RNA. Approximately 55% of trial participants had compensated cirrhosis at enrollment. At week 48, about 85% of patients with compensated cirrhosis had undetectable virus, compared with 73% of those without cirrhosis. De Backer said the difference was not necessarily statistically significant. All treated patients completed 48 weeks of therapy without discontinuations or dose interruptions, according to De Backer. She said there were no serious adverse events and one treatment-related grade three event: a decrease in neutrophil count that resolved within 15 days without interrupting treatment. Flu-like symptoms were among the more common adverse events, predominantly after the first dose. Longer-Term and Off-Treatment Data In the Phase II SOLSTICE trial, all 28 patients who remained on combination treatment through week 96 had undetectable virus, De Backer said. ALT normalization was reported in 53% of all patients at that point. An exploratory analysis examined the first 10 patients who stopped the combination after maintaining undetectable virus for at least 96 weeks. All remained on background hepatitis B therapy and had sustained virologic responses for at least 12 weeks after stopping the combination. Seven had reached 24 weeks of follow-up, and one had reached 48 weeks. Vir reported no viral rebounds or ALT flares in the group. “These finite treatment data represent an initial exploratory analysis in a small number of patients with varying durations of follow-up,” De Backer said. She added that more patients and longer follow-up are needed to assess the durability of response and the regimen’s potential as a finite or curative treatment. Regulatory Plans Vir expects top-line results from its fully enrolled ECLIPSE 2 and ECLIPSE 3 trials in the first quarter of 2027. ECLIPSE 2 is studying patients switching from bulevirtide, while ECLIPSE 3 is comparing the combination head-to-head with bulevirtide. De Backer said data from all three ECLIPSE studies are expected to support a global regulatory package, including a planned U.S. Food and Drug Administration submission in mid-2027. The company is also planning to collect additional treatment-discontinuation data from SOLSTICE and the ECLIPSE program. About Vir Biotechnology VIR Vir Biotechnology is a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company focused on developing treatments for infectious diseases and cancer. Its research draws on immunology and includes approaches such as monoclonal antibodies, small-interfering RNA therapies and T-cell-based treatments. The company's pipeline has included programs targeting chronic hepatitis B and influenza, as well as investigational cancer therapies. Vir has also pursued research and development collaborations to advance its programs. Historically, Vir partnered with GlaxoSmithKline to develop sotrovimab, an antibody treatment for COVID-19. The trial involved human participants with chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, including those with compensated cirrhosis. The ECLIPSE 1 trial enrolled 124 patients, while the SOLSTICE trial included 28 patients.

Vir Biotechnology announced that its combination treatment for chronic hepatitis delta met the primary and key secondary endpoints in the Phase III ECLIPSE 1 trial, with 79% of treated patients achieving undetectable virus at week 48. The delayed-treatment group remained untreated for 12 weeks before crossing over to the combination.
